### [SECTION READING RESPONSE / DETERMINATION]
### 1. Answers to Stored Questions for This Section

**Q3 — Share count / dividend / treasury shares / "Shares to be issued"**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D006 later notes — specifically Notes 14.1, 14.2, 15, 18, 22 — and D008)`
- **Answer & evidence**: This section (Notes 1 and 2 only, pp. 19–20) contains **no** share capital, treasury share, or dividend note. The exact shares in issue (2,434,309,216 vs ~2,333.7m implied), the 13.1% PAR Gold treasury holding, the number of "Shares to be issued" (US$148,218k for Emmerson), and whether those shares rank for the FY26 final dividend are **not addressed here**. What this section does establish: the statements are IFRS/UK-adopted IAS consistent [D006.1, physical PDF p. 19], and there is no restatement of FY25 comparatives — so any share-count reconciliation must come from the later notes. Carry Q3 forward.

**Q5 — Net cash / borrowings / leases / DMTN covenants**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for Note 16 / D008)`
- **Answer & evidence**: Notes 1–2 confirm the going-concern liquidity framing but do not break down borrowings. Key new corroboration: the Group states it had **US$78.9m of available debt facilities** and **US$246.2m of cash and cash equivalents and short-term investments** at 30 June 2026 (FY25: US$50.2m facilities; US$49.5m cash+ST investments) [D006.1, physical PDF p. 19]. This confirms the US$246,179k cash + short-term investments figure from the balance sheet. However, it does **not** explain the US$78.9m "immediately available" figure vs the US$108,312k cash + US$56.4m RCF arithmetic — that reconciliation still requires Note 13/Note 16. DMTN covenant/coupon-step-up detail not present. Carry forward.

**Q8 — PP&E accrual vs cash capex**
- **Status**: `Open (not addressed in this section)`
- **Answer & evidence**: Notes 1–2 contain no PP&E, depreciation, or capex note. Carry forward to Note 7 / D008.

**Q9 — Short-term investments composition / liquidity**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for Note 13 / D008)`
- **Answer & evidence**: The going-concern note confirms **US$246.2m** of "cash and cash equivalents and short-term investments" at 30 June 2026 [D006.1, physical PDF p. 19] — consistent with US$108,312k + US$137,867k. It does **not** describe what the US$137,867k short-term investments are (term deposits/money market/escrow) nor why "immediately available" liquidity was only US$78.9m. Note 13 still required. Carry forward.

**Q10 — Tax (Notes 6.1 & 6.2)**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (qualitative only; quantitative split stays open for Notes 6.1/6.2)`
- **Answer & evidence**: This section confirms the **South African gold-formula tax regime** is the basis for deferred tax rates, and that judgement was applied in estimating future expected deferred tax rates of mining operations, calculated at rates expected to apply when temporary differences reverse over the life of the operations [D006.1, physical PDF pp. 19–20]. This explains why the ETR (33.2%) can exceed the 28.5% prior year and why the deferred tax liability is sensitive to LoM profitability assumptions — but the current/deferred split (~US$80.4m / ~US$97.2m) and the Emmerson-vs-SA-capex composition of the US$110.0m DTL jump are **not** given here. Carry forward.

**Q11 — Share-based payments / other expenses**
- **Status**: `Open (not addressed in this section)`
- **Answer & evidence**: No SBP note in Notes 1–2. Carry forward to Note 17 / Note 3 / Note 7.

**Q20 — Receivables/payables/inventory/contract liability/acquisition balance sheet**
- **Status**: `Open (not addressed in this section)`
- **Answer & evidence**: Notes 1–2 contain no balance-sheet composition detail. Note 18 (business combinations) is the natural place for the Emmerson acquisition balance sheet. Carry forward.

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

No table footnote markers (`[^1]`, `*`, note references) appear in the captured text of pp. 19–20. The consequential qualifications are instead embedded in the going-concern and rehabilitation narrative:

**(a) Going-concern gold-price deck — embedded forecast assumption** [D006.1, physical PDF p. 19]
- Base case: **initial gold price US$4,600/oz, increasing to US$4,900/oz for the next reporting period**; semi-static thereafter; forecast horizon through **30 June 2028**.
- Downside: **US$3,680/oz** plus a **10% production reduction**.
- What it changes: This is a management-blessed price deck that is materially above spot-era planning assumptions and is the basis for the "sufficient liquidity headroom" conclusion. For forecasting, it tells us management's own downside stress is US$3,680/oz — a useful floor for scenario testing — but the base case US$4,600→4,900 is a going-concern input, not necessarily the board-approved budget price. **Must verify**: the actual budget/guidance gold price and FX deck in D008/D003 to see whether the US$4,600→4,900 path is the same deck underpinning FY27 AISC guidance (which was stated at US$/ZAR 17.00).

**(b) Liquidity qualification** [D006.1, physical PDF p. 19]
- US$78.9m available debt facilities + US$246.2m cash & ST investments at 30 June 2026.
- What it changes: It hard-codes the liquidity headroom claim but leaves the "available facilities" definition ambiguous (is the ZAR 1.0bn RCF fully undrawn/available, or partially constrained?). This is the same tension as Q9 — the US$78.9m "available facilities" is lower than the US$56.4m RCF alone would imply if fully undrawn and added to cash. **Must verify** in Note 16 / D008.

**(c) Barberton groundwater rehabilitation — unrecognised obligation** [D006.1, physical PDF p. 20]
- Amendments to the Financial Closure Provision Regulations (NEMA) require inclusion of latent/residual environmental liabilities including water pollution. A geohydrological study at Barberton indicates an **increase to the obligation of ~US$3.0m (US$0.7m discounted) in real terms**, **once the amendments become effective** — effective date yet to be determined, so **no obligation recognised at the reporting date**.
- What it changes: This is an off-balance-sheet, future-period cost step-up not in the US$34,470k rehab liability. Small in absolute terms (~US$0.7m discounted) but a reminder that the rehab provision is not fully "closed." **Must verify** in the rehab note (Note 16 area/D008) whether any sensitivity or subsequent-event disclosure updates this, and whether FY27 cash rehab funding changes.

**(d) GISTM tailings alignment — unquantified future expenditure** [D006.1, physical PDF p. 20]
- Group is **not** an ICMM member but is aligning TSFs with GISTM. Assessments ongoing; nature/scope/timing not determined; **no incremental provision recognised**, and assessments to date found no additional present obligations beyond existing provisions.
- What it changes: Potential unquantified future capex/opex at TSFs (relevant to MTR/Evander surface tailings and the calcine/tailings feed story). No number to model now, but a flag for capex contingency. **Must verify** in D008/D009 whether any TSF capex or closure cost is embedded in sustaining capex guidance.

**(e) Deferred tax rate judgement** [D006.1, physical PDF pp. 19–20]
- Rates based on "current estimate of future profitability when temporary differences will be utilised," calculated over the life of mining operations under the gold formula.
- What it changes: The 33.2% ETR and US$110.0m DTL build are highly sensitive to LoM profitability and the gold-formula marginal rate. It means the DTL is not a simple 27%/28% statutory build — future rate assumptions matter for the tax line in the forecast. **Must verify** in Note 6.2.

**(f) Impairment-test assumptions** [D006.1, physical PDF p. 20]
- CGU valuation models use internal commodity price forecasts benchmarked to analysts, LoM plans, discount rates, and include Mineral Resources (not just Reserves) where management has "high degree of confidence" in economic extraction.
- What it changes: Inclusion of Resources in cash flows is an aggressive-but-disclosed assumption; any impairment or reversal in the year would be tied to these inputs. **Must verify** in Note 7/impairment note whether any impairment was recognised or reversed in FY26 and which CGUs.

1. BASIS OF PREPARATION
The accounting policies applied in compiling the 
summarised consolidated financial statements, 
in accordance with IFRS Accounting Standards as 
issued by the IASB, are consistent with those applied in 
preparing the Group’s financial statements for the year 
ended 30 June 2025. There are no material differences 
between UK-adopted International Accounting Standards 
and IFRS Accounting Standards as applied to these 
financial statements.
The financial information set out in this announcement 
does not constitute the Company’s statutory accounts 
for the period ended 30 June 2026. 
Furthermore, these financial statements have 
been prepared in accordance with the SAICA 
Financial Reporting Guidelines as issued by the 
Accounting Practices Committee, Financial Reporting 
Pronouncements as issued by the Financial Reporting 
Standards Council, the UK Listing Rules, the JSE Listings 
Requirements and the UK Companies Act 2006.
Going concern
The Group closely monitors and manages its liquidity 
risk by means of a centralised treasury function. Cash 
forecasts are regularly produced and sensitivities run for 
different scenarios including, but not limited to, changes 
in commodity prices and different production profiles 
from the Group’s producing assets. The Group had 
US$78.9 million (FY25: US$50.2 million) of available debt 
facilities and US$246.2 million (FY25: US$49.5 million) of 
cash and cash equivalents and short-term investments 
as at 30 June 2026. The Group has considered the 
going concern forecast through to 30 June 2028, using 
a semi-static gold price in the base case scenario. 
The base case scenario assumes an initial gold price of 
US$4,600/oz increasing to US$4,900/oz for the next 
reporting period. For the downside scenario, a gold 
price of US$3,680/oz was applied, together with a 10% 
reduction in production. The Group’s forecasts, based 
on the board-approved budgets (with production in line 
with production guidance announced), demonstrate it will 
have sufficient liquidity headroom to meet its obligations, 
under both scenarios.
The board has a reasonable expectation that the Group 
has adequate resources to continue in operational 
existence for the foreseeable future. Accordingly, the 
Group continues to adopt the going concern basis 
of accounting in preparation of the 30 June 2026 
financial statements.
Alternative performance measures
The Group makes reference to APMs, in conjunction with 
IFRS Accounting Standards measures, when assessing 
its reported financial performance, financial position and 
cash flows. APMs should be considered in addition to, 
and not as a substitute for or superior to, measures of 
financial performance, financial position or cash flows 
reported in accordance with IFRS Accounting Standards. 
Further information on APMs is provided on page 72.
2. JUDGEMENTS AND ESTIMATES
The preparation of the financial statements in accordance 
with UK-adopted International Accounting Standards 
and IFRS Accounting Standards requires management to 
make judgements, estimates and assumptions that may 
materially affect the application of the Group’s accounting 
policies and the reported amounts of assets, liabilities, 
income and expenses.
These judgements and estimates are based on 
management’s best knowledge of the relevant facts 
and circumstances, historical experience, current and 
expected future economic conditions and other factors. 
Actual results may differ from the amounts included in 
the financial statements.
The estimates and underlying assumptions are reviewed 
on an ongoing basis. Revisions to accounting estimates 
are recognised prospectively.
Significant assumptions and estimates
The following areas contain information about significant 
assumptions and other sources of estimation uncertainty 
as at 30 June 2026 that have a significant risk of resulting 
in a material adjustment to the carrying amounts of 
assets and liabilities in the next reporting period.
Deferred tax rates applied within the Group
South African income tax on gold mining income is 
determined according to the gold formula that takes 
into account the taxable income and revenue from 
gold mining operations. Judgement was applied in 
determining the future expected deferred tax rates of 
the Group’s mining operations.
Deferred tax is calculated at the tax rates that are 
expected to apply to the period when the asset is 
realised, or the liability is settled, based on tax rates and 
laws that have been enacted or substantively enacted by 
the reporting date. The rates used to calculate deferred 
tax are based on the current estimate of future profitability 

--- S002 | original physical page 20 | text selection | Mixed / unspecified ---
when temporary differences will be utilised. The 
respective rates are calculated based on management’s 
best estimate through which the temporary difference will 
be realised over the life of the mining operations.
Other assumptions and estimates
Cash flow projections and key assumptions
As part of the assessment of potential impairment 
indicators, the group prepares indicative valuation models 
for certain cash generating units. Expected future cash 
flows used in discounted cash flow models are inherently 
uncertain and could materially change over time. Cash 
flow projections are significantly affected by a number of 
factors including Mineral Resources and Mineral Reserves 
together with economic factors such as commodity prices, 
foreign exchange rates and discount rates and estimates 
of production costs and future capital expenditure.
Cash flow projections are based on financial forecasts 
and LoM plans incorporating key assumptions as 
detailed below:
• Mineral Resources and Mineral Reserves:
Mineral Reserves and, where considered appropriate, 
Mineral Resources reflected within projected cash flows 
are based on Mineral Resources and Mineral Reserves 
statements (in accordance with the SAMREC Code for 
South African properties) and exploration and evaluation 
work undertaken by appropriately qualified persons. 
Mineral Resources are included where management 
has a high degree of confidence in their economic 
extraction, despite additional evaluation still being 
required prior to meeting the required confidence to 
convert to Mineral Reserves
• Commodity prices: Commodity prices are based 
on the latest internal forecasts, benchmarked with 
external sources of information, to ensure that they 
are within the range of available analyst forecasts. 
Where existing sales contracts are in place, the effects 
of such contracts or hedging arrangements are 
considered in determining future cash flows
• Discount rates: Value in use and fair value less cost 
of disposal projections are sensitive to changes in the 
discount rate
• Operating costs, capital expenditure and other 
operating factors: Operating costs and capital 
expenditure are based on financial budgets. Cash 
flow projections are based on LoM plans and internal 
management forecasts. Cost assumptions incorporate 
management experience and expectations, as well 
as the nature and location of the operation and the 
risk associated therewith (for example, the grade 
of Mineral Resources and Mineral Reserves varying 
significantly over time and unforeseen operational 
issues).
Rehabilitation obligation
The amount recognised as an obligation represents 
management’s best estimate of the consideration 
required to complete the restoration and rehabilitation 
activity. These estimates are inherently uncertain and 
could materially change over time.
At each reporting date, the Group estimates the 
environmental rehabilitation obligation. There is 
judgement in the assumptions used in determining the 
estimated obligation which include:
• closure costs, which are determined in accordance 
with regulatory requirements
• the inflation rate which has been adjusted for a
long-term view
• the risk-free rate, which is compounded annually 
and linked to the LoM
• the LoM and related Mineral Resources and 
Mineral Reserves.
An assessment of the Group’s environmental rehabilitation 
plan identified a risk relating to the potential pollution 
of groundwater at Barberton Mines. As a result of the 
amendments to the Financial Closure Provision Regulations 
promulgated in terms of the National Environmental 
Management Act, 107 of 1998, the Group is required 
to include an obligation for all latent and residual 
environmental liabilities, including water pollution, as 
part of the obligation for environmental rehabilitation and 
decommissioning costs. The Group has undertaken several 
detailed assessments, including a geohydrological study 
at Barberton Mines, to ascertain the latent and residual 
environmental liability as a result of the amendments and 
to quantify the impact of the amendments. Based on the 
current closure cost estimate, the amendments will result 
in an increase to the current obligation of approximately 
US$3.0 million (US$0.7 million on a discounted basis) for 
environmental and decommissioning costs in real terms, 
once the amendments become effective. The effective 
date of the amendments is yet to be determined. Given 
the uncertainty, no obligation has been recognised at the 
reporting date.
While the Group is not a member of the International 
Council on Mining and Metals, it continues to assess 
and progressively align the management of its TSFs 
with the requirements of the GISTM. This work includes 
assessments of the Group’s TSFs against the applicable 
GISTM requirements and the identification of actions, 
where required, to address identified gaps.
These assessments remain ongoing and the nature, 
scope and timing of any additional measures that may be 
required have not yet been fully determined. Accordingly, 
at the reporting date, the Group is not able to reliably 
estimate any incremental future expenditure that may 
arise specifically from further alignment with the GISTM. 
Furthermore, the assessments completed to date have 
not identified additional present obligations requiring 
recognition beyond those already incorporated, where 
applicable, in the Group’s existing rehabilitation and closure 
provisions. No additional provision has therefore been 
recognised specifically in respect of GISTM alignment.
The Group will continue to assess the implications of the 
GISTM as the programme progresses and will recognise 
or disclose any additional obligations in accordance with 
the applicable accounting requirements when sufficient 
information becomes available. Further information 
regarding the Group’s tailings management practices and 
progress towards GISTM conformance is available on 
our website at https//www.panafricanresources.com/
pan-african-resources-tailings-management-system/

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

No table footnote markers (`[^1]`, `*`, note references) appear in the captured text of pp. 19–20. The consequential qualifications are instead embedded in the going-concern and rehabilitation narrative:

**(a) Going-concern gold-price deck — embedded forecast assumption** [D006.1, physical PDF p. 19]
- Base case: **initial gold price US$4,600/oz, increasing to US$4,900/oz for the next reporting period**; semi-static thereafter; forecast horizon through **30 June 2028**.
- Downside: **US$3,680/oz** plus a **10% production reduction**.
- What it changes: This is a management-blessed price deck that is materially above spot-era planning assumptions and is the basis for the "sufficient liquidity headroom" conclusion. For forecasting, it tells us management's own downside stress is US$3,680/oz — a useful floor for scenario testing — but the base case US$4,600→4,900 is a going-concern input, not necessarily the board-approved budget price. **Must verify**: the actual budget/guidance gold price and FX deck in D008/D003 to see whether the US$4,600→4,900 path is the same deck underpinning FY27 AISC guidance (which was stated at US$/ZAR 17.00).

**(b) Liquidity qualification** [D006.1, physical PDF p. 19]
- US$78.9m available debt facilities + US$246.2m cash & ST investments at 30 June 2026.
- What it changes: It hard-codes the liquidity headroom claim but leaves the "available facilities" definition ambiguous (is the ZAR 1.0bn RCF fully undrawn/available, or partially constrained?). This is the same tension as Q9 — the US$78.9m "available facilities" is lower than the US$56.4m RCF alone would imply if fully undrawn and added to cash. **Must verify** in Note 16 / D008.

**(c) Barberton groundwater rehabilitation — unrecognised obligation** [D006.1, physical PDF p. 20]
- Amendments to the Financial Closure Provision Regulations (NEMA) require inclusion of latent/residual environmental liabilities including water pollution. A geohydrological study at Barberton indicates an **increase to the obligation of ~US$3.0m (US$0.7m discounted) in real terms**, **once the amendments become effective** — effective date yet to be determined, so **no obligation recognised at the reporting date**.
- What it changes: This is an off-balance-sheet, future-period cost step-up not in the US$34,470k rehab liability. Small in absolute terms (~US$0.7m discounted) but a reminder that the rehab provision is not fully "closed." **Must verify** in the rehab note (Note 16 area/D008) whether any sensitivity or subsequent-event disclosure updates this, and whether FY27 cash rehab funding changes.

**(d) GISTM tailings alignment — unquantified future expenditure** [D006.1, physical PDF p. 20]
- Group is **not** an ICMM member but is aligning TSFs with GISTM. Assessments ongoing; nature/scope/timing not determined; **no incremental provision recognised**, and assessments to date found no additional present obligations beyond existing provisions.
- What it changes: Potential unquantified future capex/opex at TSFs (relevant to MTR/Evander surface tailings and the calcine/tailings feed story). No number to model now, but a flag for capex contingency. **Must verify** in D008/D009 whether any TSF capex or closure cost is embedded in sustaining capex guidance.

**(e) Deferred tax rate judgement** [D006.1, physical PDF pp. 19–20]
- Rates based on "current estimate of future profitability when temporary differences will be utilised," calculated over the life of mining operations under the gold formula.
- What it changes: The 33.2% ETR and US$110.0m DTL build are highly sensitive to LoM profitability and the gold-formula marginal rate. It means the DTL is not a simple 27%/28% statutory build — future rate assumptions matter for the tax line in the forecast. **Must verify** in Note 6.2.

**(f) Impairment-test assumptions** [D006.1, physical PDF p. 20]
- CGU valuation models use internal commodity price forecasts benchmarked to analysts, LoM plans, discount rates, and include Mineral Resources (not just Reserves) where management has "high degree of confidence" in economic extraction.
- What it changes: Inclusion of Resources in cash flows is an aggressive-but-disclosed assumption; any impairment or reversal in the year would be tied to these inputs. **Must verify** in Note 7/impairment note whether any impairment was recognised or reversed in FY26 and which CGUs.

3. SEGMENT ANALYSIS
Operating segments are reported in a manner consistent 
with the internal reporting provided to the chief operating 
decision-maker. The chief operating decision-maker, 
which is responsible for allocating resources and 
assessing performance of the operating segments, has 
been identified as the Pan African executive committee 
(Exco). The operating segments of the Group are 
determined based on the reports used to make strategic 
decisions that are reviewed by Exco. Exco considers 
the business principally according to the location and 
nature of the products and services provided, with each 
segment representing a strategic business unit.
The reported segments comprise the following:
Mining operations
These segments derive their revenue from mining, 
extraction, production and the sale of gold.
South African operations
• Barberton Mines including the BTRP located in 
Barberton
• Evander Mines: Elikhulu, the underground 8 Shaft 
pillar, 24, 25 and 26 Level project, Egoli project and 
surface sources) located in Evander
• MTR operation: The MTR operation is located in 
the Mogale district. The plant was commissioned in 
October 2024 to process gold tailings deposits of 
Mogale Gold Proprietary Limited (Mogale Gold) and 
Mintails SA Soweto Cluster Proprietary Limited (MSC)
• Solar projects consist of the solar plant located at 
Evander Mines, the solar plant at Barberton Mines and 
the extension of Evander Mines’ solar plant. The solar 
plants were sold at arm’s length to the respective mines 
on 30 June 2026. Following completion of the disposal, 
the Group no longer has any operating activities or 
assets attributable to this segment and the segment 
will no longer be separately reported from 1 July 2026.
Australian operations
• Tennant Mines is located in the Northern Territory 
and Yungatha Asset Holdings Proprietary Limited 
(Yungatha) complements the Group’s current portfolio 
of high-margin, long-life surface remining operations. 
The segment includes Yungatha, which operates 
a motel in the Tennant Creek region to support the 
workforce requirements of local mining companies 
and other contractors, including Tennant Consolidated 
Mining Group Proprietary Limited (Tennant company) 
employees. In addition, the segment also includes 
Emmerson as of 22 June 2026; refer to note 18.1. 
Emmerson is a mineral exploration and royalty 
company engaged in the exploration and evaluation 
of mineral properties. The segment results include 
the assets and liabilities of Emmerson. No income or 
expenses have been included in the segment results 
for the reporting period, as there was no material 
income or expenditure arising from Emmerson 
between the acquisition date and the reporting date.
Other operations
• Exploration assets consist of five prospecting 
concessions (or exploration licences) in north-eastern 
Sudan (the Block 12 concessions), covering an area 
of almost 1,100km² and located approximately 70km 
north-west of Port Sudan
• Agricultural ESG projects mainly comprise the 
Group’s Barberton Blueberries project (Barberton 
Blue Proprietary Limited), as well as other small￾scale agricultural projects in Barberton Mines’ host 
community areas
• Corporate consists mainly of the Group’s holding 
companies and management services company 
which renders services to the Group and is located in 
Johannesburg
• Pan African Resources Funding Company Limited 
(Funding Company) is the centralised treasury 
function of the Group located in Johannesburg.

### [SECTION READING RESPONSE / DETERMINATION]
# Forensic Analysis — D006.2 Note 3 Segment Analysis (pp. 20–24)

---

### 1. Answers to Stored Questions for This Section

**Q3 (High) — Exact shares in issue / treasury / "Shares to be issued" (Emmerson) / dividend ranking**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D006 later notes 14.1/14.2/15/18/22)`
- **Answer & evidence**: This section confirms the *accounting* treatment of the Emmerson transaction but **not the share count**. Segment note states Emmerson was consolidated "as of 22 June 2026" and that "No income or expenses have been included in the segment results for the reporting period, as there was no material income or expenditure arising from Emmerson between the acquisition date and the reporting date" `[D006.2, p. 20]`. Segment assets at p. 22 show Australian segment assets (Tennant + Emmerson) of US$345,073k vs FY25 US$122,945k `[D006.2, pp. 22, 24]`, and geographical non-current assets Australia US$365,207k vs US$155,716k `[D006.2, p. 24]` — consistent with a large Emmerson asset injection, but the note does **not** disclose share count, the US$148,218k "shares to be issued," or dividend ranking. Still open — must check Notes 14.1, 14.2, 15, 18.1, 22.

**Q8 (Low-Med) — PP&E accrual additions vs cash capex**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved`
- **Answer & evidence**: Footnote [^2] p. 22 defines segment **capital expenditure** as "additions to property, plant and equipment, mineral rights and exploration assets" — i.e., it is an **accrual/addition** measure, not cash. Group total capex per segment note = **US$223,995k** `[D006.2, p. 22]`, which reconciles closely to the US$224.0m accrual additions referenced in Q8. The US$215.8m cash capex gap (~US$8.2m) is therefore a working-capital/creditor timing difference; the segment note does not itself quantify capitalised working costs at Evander/Tennant. Remainder open for Note 7.

**Q9 (High) — Short-term investments / "immediately available" liquidity**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved`
- **Answer & evidence**: Segment note shows **Funding Company** segment assets US$189,606k and net assets US$137,658k `[D006.2, p. 22]`, and Funding Company revenue US$114,977k (intra-group dividends) `[D006.2, p. 21]`. This confirms the treasury entity holds material financial assets but does **not** break out the US$137,867k short-term investments or the US$78.9m "immediately available" figure. RCF availability at 30 June 2026 still open — target Note 13/16.

**Q10 / Q21 (High) — Tax split, ETR, DTL composition**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved`
- **Answer & evidence**: Segment tax charge by operation `[D006.2, p. 21]`:
  - Barberton (50,997); Evander (77,439); Solar (1,996); MTR (38,605); Tennant (8,512); Exploration/Agri/Corporate +809; Funding Company (717). **Group total (177,457)**.
  - Implied ETR = 177,457 / 534,368 PBT = **33.2%** `[D006.2, p. 21]`. Note the **Corporate line carries a tax credit of +809** and **Funding Company a (717) charge** — intra-group interest/dividend tax effects that must be understood in the ETR reconciliation (Q21).
  - FY25 comparatives: tax (56,028) on PBT 196,633 = **28.5%** ETR `[D006.2, p. 23]` — a ~4.7pp ETR step-up YoY that needs explaining (Emmerson DTL, SA capex allowance utilisation, gold-formula tax).
  - **MTR effective tax**: 38,605 / 139,116 = **27.8%**; **Tennant**: 8,512 / 23,836 = **35.7%** (Australian rate ~30% + adjustments); **Evander**: 77,439 / 275,377 = **28.1%**; **Barberton**: 50,997 / 158,346 = **32.2%**. The Barberton rate being above SA statutory 27% is notable and must be checked against Note 6 (gold-formula tax / non-deductibles).
  - The **US$110.0m DTL jump** and current/deferred split are **not** in this section — still open for D006.1/Note 6.

**Q11 (High) — SBP / Other expenses split; cash-settled SBP**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved`
- **Answer & evidence**: Segment note shows **Other expenses** by segment `[D006.2, p. 21]`: Barberton (9,711); Evander (5,141); Solar (78); MTR (8,473); Tennant (9,432); **Corporate (52,443)**; Funding (231); total **(85,958)**. The Corporate US$52,443k is the dominant driver and is consistent with the US$85,958k "Other expenses" referenced in Q11 — this is where SBP MTM and Emmerson transaction costs likely sit. Footnote [^1] p. 21 confirms: "Other expenses and income exclude intra-Group management fees. Finance income and finance costs exclude intra-Group interest." It does **not** split SBP vs Emmerson costs — still open for Note 17.
- **Cross-check**: Total "Other expenses" (85,958) + "Other income" 4,596 = net other expense 81,362; the Corporate line alone (52,443) is ~61% of group other expenses — a critical forecast line.

**Q20 (Medium) — Receivables/payables/contract liability/inventory/Emmerson balance sheet**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved`
- **Answer & evidence**: Segment note confirms Emmerson **assets and liabilities are included** from 22 June 2026 with **no P&L** `[D006.2, p. 20]`, and Australian segment assets jumped to US$345,073k (from US$122,945k) with liabilities US$75,204k (from US$64,757k) `[D006.2, pp. 22, 24]`. This is the balance-sheet footprint of the acquisition, but the detailed composition (receivables US$41,654k, payables US$142,294k, contract liability US$8,030k, inventory) is **not** broken out here — still open for Notes 9–12, 18.

**Q22 (High) — Barberton groundwater NEMA rehabilitation**
- **Status**: `Open — not addressed in this section`
- **Answer & evidence**: No rehabilitation/provision detail in the segment note. Carry forward to rehab note (D006.1 p. 20 / later notes).

**Q23 (Medium) — Impairment/reversal by CGU**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved`
- **Answer & evidence**: Segment note shows **impairment loss on non-financial assets of US$345k** in FY26, all in **Agricultural ESG projects** `[D006.2, p. 21]` (no mining CGU impairment). FY25 comparative: US$2,954k impairment, split Evander (27) and Exploration assets (2,927) `[D006.2, p. 23]`. So FY26 mining CGUs were **not** impaired at the segment level; any CGU-level impairment test/pricing assumptions still to be confirmed in Note 7/impairment note.

**Q25 (Medium) — Facility headroom / covenants / maturities**
- **Status**: `Open — not addressed in this section`
- **Answer & evidence**: Segment note shows Funding Company net assets US$137,658k and segment liabilities US$51,948k `[D006.2, p. 22]`, but no facility headroom, drawn amounts, covenants, or maturities. Carry forward to Note 16 / D008.

**Q26 (Low-Med) — TSF/GISTM closure costs in sustaining capex/rehab**
- **Status**: `Open — not addressed in this section`
- **Answer & evidence**: No TSF/GISTM detail. Carry forward.

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

**Footnote [^1] (p. 21) — "Other expenses and income exclude intra-Group management fees. Finance income and finance costs exclude intra-Group interest."**
- **What it changes**: The segment "Other expenses" line of (85,958) and "Other income" of 4,596 are **stated before** intra-group management fees, which are shown separately lower in the table (Management fees row, p. 21: Barberton (5,687), Evander (2,831), Solar (237), MTR (556), Tennant (3,188), Corporate +12,860, Funding (272), net nil). This means:
  1. The **Corporate US$52,443k other expense is NOT inflated by management-fee recharges** — it is a genuine group-level cost pool (likely SBP MTM + Emmerson transaction costs + corporate overheads).
  2. Segment profitability **before** the management-fee allocation overstates mining-segment margins and understates Corporate; for forecasting, use the "Profit/(loss) after tax including intra-Group transactions" row (p. 21) as the true economic segment result.
  3. The **Funding Company finance income of 4,891 and finance costs of (11,184)** are stated excluding intra-group interest — the large net funding cost at Funding Company (net ~(6,293) before the intra-group reallocation shown in the second block, which adds +8,695 finance income to Funding) must be reconciled to the group net finance cost of ~(11,470) (5,848 − 17,318) `[D006.2, p. 21]`. **Verification needed in Note 5 / Note 16**: confirm the external vs intra-group interest split and that no double-count arises.

**Footnote [^1] (p. 22) — "The segment assets and liabilities above exclude intra-Group balances."**
- **What it changes**: Segment net assets (total US$1,017,559k) exclude intra-group balances. The sum of segment net assets **plus goodwill** (18,438) = 1,036.0m, which should reconcile to group equity — but intra-group eliminations mean this is **not** a direct equity reconciliation. For forecasting, the **geographical non-current assets** table (p. 24: SA 911,962; Australia 365,207; Other 480; total 1,277,649) is the cleaner asset-base anchor.

**Footnote [^2] (p. 22) — "Capital expenditure comprises additions to property, plant and equipment, mineral rights and exploration assets."**
- **What it changes**: Confirms segment capex (US$223,995k) is an **accrual additions** measure, not cash. This directly explains the US$224.0m vs US$215.8m cash capex gap in Q8 (~US$8.2m creditor timing). **Verification**: Note 7 must confirm the accrual-to-cash bridge and whether any **capitalised working costs** at Evander/Tennant sit inside this US$223,995k.

**Footnote [^3] (p. 22) — "Adjusted EBITDA comprises earnings before interest, tax, depreciation and amortisation adjusted for impairment losses on non-financial assets."**
- **What it changes**: FY26 Adjusted EBITDA = **US$609,431k** `[D006.2, p. 22]`. Crucially, the FY26 definition **only adjusts for impairment** (345) and loss/profit on disposal (781) — it does **NOT** adjust for SBP, Emmerson transaction costs, or unrealised derivatives. Contrast FY25 footnote [^4] (p. 24), which **also** adjusted for **bargain purchase gains (28,019)** and **unrealised fair value losses on derivatives (1,925)** — i.e., the **definition of Adjusted EBITDA changed between FY25 and FY26**. This is a material comparability flag:
  1. FY25 Adjusted EBITDA = 226,584; FY26 = 609,431 — a 169% jump, but on a **different definition**.
  2. The FY26 Corporate other expense of (52,443) is **not** added back in Adjusted EBITDA — so if it contains SBP MTM and one-off Emmerson costs, Adjusted EBITDA understates "clean" EBITDA. **Verification in D008 (APM reconciliation)**: confirm whether SBP/Emmerson costs are excluded from the APM "Adjusted EBITDA" headline and whether the definition change is disclosed.
- **Priority flag**: This is a **high-priority** finding — definition change + large Corporate cost pool not adjusted = forecast risk.

**Footnote [^1] (p. 23) — Tennant Mines FY25 partial period**: "Tennant company was acquired in November 2024 and the results are for an eight-month period. Yungatha was acquired in December 2024 and the results are for a seven-month period."
- **What it changes**: FY25 Tennant revenue of only US$3,873k and **bargain purchase gain of US$28,019k** are **partial-period and one-off**. FY26 Tennant revenue US$132,804k is the first full year — so the FY25→FY26 Tennant revenue jump is **not** organic growth; do not annualise FY25. The bargain purchase gain (28,019) is non-recurring and must be stripped from any run-rate. **Verification in D009**: confirm Tennant FY26 full-year tonnes/grade/recovery to model FY27.

**Other embedded qualifications (p. 20):**
- **Solar projects sold 30 June 2026**: "The solar plants were sold at arm's length to the respective mines on 30 June 2026. Following completion of the disposal, the Group no longer has any operating activities or assets attributable to this segment and the segment will no longer be separately reported from 1 July 2026." → **FY27 solar segment = nil**; the FY26 solar loss (2,448) and capex (7,159) disappear. Also implies a **disposal** — check for gain/loss on disposal and any continuing lease/PPA costs in Note 7/16.
- **Emmerson no P&L**: confirms FY26 group P&L excludes Emmerson operations — FY27 will include a **full year of Emmerson** (exploration/royalty), which is a step-up not visible in FY26 numbers.
- **MTR**: "plant was commissioned in October 2024" — FY26 is the **first full year** of MTR (revenue 228,268 vs FY25 87,344). FY25 partial-year; do not annualise.

## 1. Answers to Stored Questions for This Section

**Q35 (Medium) — Note 4 revenue: reconcile segment revenue total 1,156,451 to group revenue; check MTR/third-party classification and column alignment.**
- **Status:** `Resolved`
- **Answer & evidence:** Note 4 total revenue of **US$1,156,451k** ties exactly to the segment revenue total of **1,156,451** carried from D006.2. The disaggregation confirms the composition: gold 1,152,437 + silver 2,697 + copper 723 + blueberries 594 = 1,156,451 (arithmetic checks). Geographical split: South Africa 1,023,299 + Australia 132,804 + UK/Europe 328 + US 20 = 1,156,451 (checks). Note the Australia line of **132,804** exactly equals the Tennant FY26 revenue figure flagged in Q34 — confirming Tennant is the entire Australian revenue line and that Emmerson (consolidated 22 June 2026) contributed **nil** revenue in FY26. [D006.3, physical PDF p. 25]

**Q20 (Medium) — Note 4/contract liability: nature of US$8,030k contract liability.**
- **Status:** `Partially resolved` (composition now clear; balance-sheet classification and cash-flow treatment still open)
- **Answer & evidence:** The US$8,030k contract liability is **advance consideration received** in FY26, comprising: (i) Barberton forward sale contract of **US$4.5m** with RMB, settled in physical bullion **July 2026** (post-year-end); and (ii) Tennant advance payment for gold sale of **US$3.5m** at 30 June 2026, gold collected **July 2026**. 4.5 + 3.5 = 8.0 ≈ 8,030 (small rounding/FX residual — flag). Both are current and both settle in July 2026, so the liability unwinds in FY27Q1 with **no FY27 cash inflow** (already received). Roll-forward: opening nil + advance 8,030 + interest nil − revenue recognised nil − FX nil = 8,030 closing (checks). The prior-year contract liability (2023 RMB forward, 4,846oz/month at ZAR1,025,000/kg = US$1,723/oz, 24 months) was fully fulfilled in FY25 (recognised as revenue 15,812 in FY25). **Still open:** confirm these advances are classified as operating cash flow in Note 19 and that the July 2026 physical delivery does not create a FY27 revenue double-count. [D006.3, physical PDF p. 25]

**Q31 (High) — Note 16/Note 5: reconcile Funding Company finance costs (11,184) and income 4,891 ex-intra-group to group net finance cost; external debt/lease/DMTN/RCF interest.**
- **Status:** `Partially resolved` (group-level Note 5 now captured; Funding Company ex-intra-group split still open)
- **Answer & evidence:** Note 5 group totals: finance income **5,848** (FY25 1,856); finance costs **(17,318)** (FY25 (21,073)); net finance costs **(11,470)** (FY25 (19,217)). Key components: borrowings interest **(14,505)** vs FY25 (25,033) — a **10,528 reduction**, consistent with debt repayment; lease liabilities (441); environmental rehabilitation obligation (2,025); trade payables/financial liabilities/tax/cash small. Finance income: cash 4,442; restricted cash 60; tax authorities 177; other 27; **fair value gain on short-term investments 1,142** (Note 13 — new in FY26, confirms short-term investments are marked to FV through P&L). **Critical flag:** borrowing costs capitalised is **nil in FY26 vs +7,190 in FY25** — i.e., FY25 finance costs were *reduced* by 7,190 of capitalised interest; FY26 has none. This distorts the YoY borrowing-cost comparison: gross borrowing cost fell from ~32,223 (25,033+7,190) to 14,505, a much larger true decline. **Still open:** tie the 14,505 to external instruments (DMTN/RCF/other) and reconcile to the Funding Company segment line (finance costs 11,184, income 4,891) net of intra-group eliminations. [D006.4, physical PDF p. 25; D006.2 p. 21]

## 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

- **Footnote [^1] — "Based on customer country of origin"** [D006.3, p. 25]. This changes the interpretation of the geographical table: the South Africa 1,023,299 / Australia 132,804 split reflects **where the customer is domiciled, not where the metal is mined**. Implication: the Australian 132,804 is Tennant's revenue (Tennant is Australian-domiciled), and the SA line bundles all South African operations plus any offshore sales to SA-domiciled traders/refiners. It does **not** support a clean geographic margin or FX-exposure read. Verification required: confirm in D008/D009 whether Tennant's 132,804 equals its full FY26 revenue and whether any Barberton/Evander ounces are sold to non-SA customers (which would sit in UK/Europe 328 or US 20 — both immaterial, so effectively all SA-mined gold is sold to SA-domiciled customers).

- **Contract liability narrative (embedded qualification)** [D006.3, p. 25]. The two advances (Barberton US$4.5m, Tennant US$3.5m) both settle in **July 2026**, i.e., FY27Q1, by **physical bullion delivery**. Forecast implications: (i) the US$8,030k is *already-received cash* — it must **not** be added to FY27 operating cash flow again when the gold is delivered; (ii) it represents ~8,030/… ounces of FY27 gold committed at contract (not spot) terms — check whether the Barberton forward was struck at a price below the FY27 budget deck (margin drag); (iii) it slightly reduces FY27 saleable spot ounces. Verification: D008/D009 for July 2026 delivery price vs budget, and Note 19 (operating cash flow) to confirm no double-count.

- **"Borrowing costs capitalised – nil (FY25: 7,190)"** [D006.4, p. 25]. This is a small-print line that materially changes the finance-cost trend read: FY25 net finance costs were flattered (costs reduced) by 7,190 of capitalised interest on qualifying assets (likely the solar build / MTR or Evander expansion). FY26 has **zero capitalisation**, so the 14,505 borrowing cost is fully expensed. Forecast implication: FY27 finance cost run-rate should be modelled off the **fully-expensed** 14,505 base (adjusted for debt paydown), **not** the FY25 25,033 which included capitalised offsets. Verification: Note 7/Note 16 — confirm no FY27 qualifying-asset capitalisation (e.g., if a new expansion starts, capitalised interest could reappear and reduce P&L cost).

- **"Fair value gain on short-term investments 1,142" (Note 13)** [D006.4, p. 25]. Confirms short-term investments are FVTPL and contributed 1,142 of non-cash finance income in FY26. Forecast implication: this is volatile/mark-to-market and should be **excluded from sustainable net finance cost**; flag for the D008 APM reconciliation (is it in "adjusted" earnings or excluded?). Ties to Q9 (composition of US$137,867k short-term investments).

- **No missing footnote markers:** both [^1] and the Note 13 cross-reference have their definitions present in the captured evidence. No footnote text is missing from this capture.

### [SECTION READING RESPONSE / DETERMINATION]
### 1. Answers to Stored Questions for This Section

**Q10 (High): Notes 6.1/6.2 — current vs deferred tax split (~US$80.4m/~US$97.2m); ETR reconciliation; unredeemed capex shields; composition of US$110.0m DTL jump (Emmerson vs SA capex allowances); deferred tax rate(s) used.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D006 later notes / D008)`
- **Answer & evidence**: Note 6.1 confirms the current/deferred split precisely: total income tax expense US$177,457k = SA current tax 73,486 (current year 73,551, prior year (65)) + Australian current tax 6,799 (current year 6,817, prior year (18)) + deferred tax 97,172 (current year 97,071, prior year 101). Total current tax = 80,285; total deferred = 97,172 — consistent with the ~80.4/97.2 prior expectation (small rounding to prior-year true-ups). `[D006.4, physical PDF p. 25]`. The DTL jump is confirmed at 109,974 (140,506 → 250,480), driven overwhelmingly by PP&E temporary differences rising from 136,460 to 255,589 (+119,129), partly offset by inventory DTL falling 9,080 → 2,577. `[D006.4, physical PDF p. 26]`. Deferred tax rates applied are now explicit: Barberton 28.29% (FY25 24.00%), Evander other/mining rights 29.03% (28.00%), MTR 29.95% (28.00%), Tennant 30.00% (30.00%), Other Group 27.00% (27.00%). `[D006.4, physical PDF p. 26]`. **Still open**: ETR reconciliation (33.2% vs 28.5%) is not shown in this capture — no tax-rate reconciliation table, no unredeemed capex shield disclosure, and no split of the PP&E DTL increase between Emmerson acquisition, SA capex allowances, and FCTR. The FCTR movement of +13,083 on DTL is a non-P&L item and must be excluded from the tax charge when modelling.

**Q21 (High): Notes 6.1/6.2 — gold-formula SA tax, deferred tax rates by jurisdiction, ETR reconciliation, DTL roll-forward.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (ETR reconciliation still open)`
- **Answer & evidence**: Deferred tax rates by jurisdiction are now captured (see Q10 above) `[D006.4, physical PDF p. 26]`. The DTL roll-forward is captured: opening 140,506 + acquisition 0 + P&L 97,377 + transfer from DTA (486) + FCTR 13,083 = closing 250,480. Note the P&L deferred movement in the roll-forward (97,377) differs from the Note 6.1 deferred tax expense line (97,172) by 205 — which is exactly the DTA P&L movement (205) netted in the DTA reconciliation (2,072 + 205 − 486 + 130 = 1,921). So group net deferred tax P&L = 97,377 − 205 = 97,172, which ties. `[D006.4, physical PDF p. 26]`. **Still open**: no ETR reconciliation table in this capture; no explicit gold-formula tax mechanic; no split of the PP&E DTL increase between Emmerson and SA capex allowances.

**Q29 (High): [NEW] Note 6 tax — reconcile ETR 33.2% FY26 vs 28.5% FY25; Barberton 32.2%, Tennant 35.7%; Corporate tax credit +809, Funding (717).**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (ETR reconciliation still open)`
- **Answer & evidence**: The absolute tax charge and split are confirmed (177,457 total; 80,285 current; 97,172 deferred) `[D006.4, physical PDF p. 25]`. The **statutory deferred tax rate increases** at Barberton (24.00% → 28.29%), Evander (28.00% → 29.03%) and MTR (28.00% → 29.95%) are a material driver of the higher deferred tax charge and hence the ETR step-up, and this is now evidenced. `[D006.4, physical PDF p. 26]`. **Still open**: the formal ETR reconciliation (33.2% vs 28.5%) is not in this capture; the segment effective rates (Barberton 32.2%, Tennant 35.7%) and Corporate/Funding tax lines cannot be reconciled here. The rate changes above are a partial explanation but the numeric bridge must be checked in a later D006 note or D008.

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

**Footnote [^1] — DTL side (p. 26):** *"In the previous reporting period, employee obligations, cash-settled share-based payment obligations and the expected credit loss allowance were incorrectly classified as an environmental rehabilitation obligation. These items have been disaggregated in the current reporting period, with comparative figures restated accordingly."*
- **What it changes**: This is a **prior-period misclassification correction**, not a change in totals. It means the FY25 comparative DTL line items cannot be taken at face value from any earlier-stated version — the employee obligations (−2,283), cash-settled SBP (−1,768) and ECL (−48) lines are now shown separately and were previously buried inside "environmental rehabilitation obligation." Critically, the **total net DTL (140,506 FY25) is unaffected** because it is a reclassification within liabilities. For forecasting, the important point is that the **cash-settled SBP deferred tax asset/liability is real and separately tracked** — DTL side −4,715 (FY26) vs −1,768 (FY25), and DTA side +1,065 (FY26) vs +125 (FY25). The swing in the cash-settled SBP temporary difference (roughly −2,947 DTL and +940 DTA year-on-year) is consistent with a large FY26 SBP charge/accrual, which ties to open Q11/Q28 (SBP expense split). **Verification needed**: confirm in Note 17 the FY26 cash-settled SBP expense that generated this temporary difference, and confirm no restated total changed.

**Footnote [^2] — DTA side (p. 26):** *"Other payables previously included the temporary difference arising from the accrual for employee benefits and leave pay liability. This amount has been disaggregated in the current reporting period, with comparative figures restated accordingly."*
- **What it changes**: Same theme — another **prior-period disaggregation** of the DTA "other payables" line (FY26 91 vs FY25 545), with employee obligations now shown at 789 (FY26) vs 626 (FY25). Again a reclassification, not a total change. Flag that any model built off FY25 comparative DTA detail must use the **restated** figures, not pre-restatement ones.

**Unmarked qualification (p. 26, DTA note):** *"Deferred tax assets have only been recognised, where applicable, on the basis that the individual Group companies will be able to generate future taxable economic benefits to utilise current deductible temporary differences."*
- **What it changes**: This is a **recognition caveat** — DTAs are only recognised where future taxable profits are expected. The very small net DTA (1,921) and the near-elimination of the assessed-loss DTA (6,729 → 3) imply the group has **largely exhausted recognised tax losses** and is now a full taxpayer on most SA/Australian profits. For forecasting, this supports a **structurally higher cash tax rate going forward** (no large loss shields), consistent with the ETR step-up. **Verification needed**: check whether any unrecognised assessed losses / unredeemed capex remain (Q10) that could shield FY27 cash tax.

**Arithmetic checks performed (no tension found, but flagged for follow-up):**
- Current tax 80,285 + deferred 97,172 = 177,457 ✓ ties to Note 6.1 total.
- DTL roll-forward: 140,506 + 0 + 97,377 − 486 + 13,083 = 250,480 ✓.
- DTA roll-forward: 2,072 + 205 − 486 + 130 = 1,921 ✓.
- Net deferred P&L: 97,377 (DTL) − 205 (DTA) = 97,172 ✓ ties to Note 6.1.
- DTL line-item sum check: 2,577 + 255,589 − 2,628 − 4,715 − 56 − 172 + 34 + 0 − 637 + 569 − 81 = **250,480** ✓ (assessed loss nil in FY26). **Note**: "Net deferred tax liabilities" of 250,480 is stated before netting the 1,921 DTA; the true net deferred tax position is **248,559** — this presentation convention must be respected when modelling the balance sheet.

### [SECTION READING RESPONSE / DETERMINATION]
### 1. Answers to Stored Questions for This Section

**Q8 (Low-Med): Note 7 PP&E — reconcile US$224.0m accrual additions vs US$215.8m cash capex; capitalised working costs at Evander/Tennant.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D005.1 / D006 later notes / D008)`
- **Answer & evidence**: Note 7 confirms total accrual **Additions = 223,995** [D006.5, p. 27], which ties exactly to the segment capex figure of 223,995 captured earlier [D006.2 p. 22]. The **cash capex of 215.8m is not shown in Note 7** — Note 7 is an accrual roll-forward and does not include a cash-payment reconciliation. The ~8.2m accrual-vs-cash bridge therefore **cannot be closed from this section**. Also note: no line item in Note 7 explicitly labels "capitalised working costs" — additions are split only by asset class (mineral rights 12,431 + 26,030; buildings 14,907; plant & machinery 44,824; right-of-use 2,505 + 4,667; capital work under construction 111,542; shafts 5,852; other 1,237). Whether working costs at Evander/Tennant are embedded in these classes (likely capital work under construction 111,542) requires the cash-flow statement (D005.1) and Note 19. **Still open.**

**Q23 (Medium): Impairment note (likely Note 7) — any FY26 impairment/reversal and CGUs; price/discount-rate assumptions incl. Mineral Resources in cash flows.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (assumptions still open for D008 / later notes)`
- **Answer & evidence**: Note 7 accumulated depreciation confirms **FY26 impairment losses = (345)**, all in the "Capital work under construction" column [D006.5, p. 28], consistent with the earlier 345 Agricultural impairment [D006.2 p. 21]. **No mining CGU impairment or reversal in FY26.** No impairment-test assumptions (gold price, discount rate, Mineral Resources inclusion) appear in this PP&E note — those must be checked in D008 (APMs) or any separate impairment note. **Assumptions still open.**

**Q30 (High): Note 7 PP&E / Note 5 — accrual capex 223,995 vs cash 215.8m bridge (~8.2m); capitalised working costs Evander/Tennant; solar disposal accounting.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (bridge and solar accounting still open)`
- **Answer & evidence**: Accrual additions confirmed at **223,995** [D006.5, p. 27]. Note 7 does **not** disclose a cash bridge, so the ~8.2m accrual/cash difference remains unresolved here. **Solar disposal accounting**: Note 7 shows FY26 **Disposals cost (2,301)** and accumulated depreciation released **376**, plus a **Derecognition** line (cost (4,301); accumulated depreciation released 3,725) [D006.5, pp. 27-28]. The derecognition of right-of-use assets (buildings RoU (2,953); plant RoU (54); CWUC (717); other (577)) strongly suggests lease/asset retirements, possibly linked to the solar plants sold 30 June 2026 — but the note does **not** name the solar assets or show a disposal gain/loss. **Solar gain/loss and proceeds still open for D005.1 / D008.**

**Q33 (Medium): Note 7 / disposal — solar plants sold 30 June 2026; disposal gain/loss, proceeds, retained obligations; FY27 solar cost elimination (FY26 loss 2,448, capex 7,159).**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (gain/loss and proceeds still open)`
- **Answer & evidence**: Note 7 shows total FY26 **disposals at cost (2,301)** with **accumulated depreciation released 376** (net book value disposed ≈ 1,925) and a separate **derecognition** of cost (4,301) with accumulated depreciation released 3,725 (net NBV ≈ 576) [D006.5, pp. 27-28]. Neither line is labelled "solar," and **no disposal gain/loss or proceeds are disclosed in Note 7**. The solar sale gain/loss and cash proceeds must be confirmed in D005.1 (cash flow / disposals note) or D008. **Still open.**

**Q43 (High): [NEW] split PP&E DTL increase 136,460→255,589 (+119,129) between Emmerson acquisition, SA capex allowances/unredeemed capex, rate re-measurement, FCTR.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved — indirect evidence only (full split still open for the deferred-tax note / D008)`
- **Answer & evidence**: Note 7 gives the **accounting** PP&E movement but **not** the tax-base split. However, it supplies key inputs: **Additions through acquisitions[^1] = 142,436**, almost entirely **mineral rights 142,244** [D006.5, p. 27], with footnote [^1] pointing to **Note 18** (Emmerson). This confirms the bulk of the acquired PP&E (and therefore likely the acquired PP&E deferred-tax liability) relates to Emmerson mineral rights. The remaining PP&E DTL increase must come from SA capex allowances (additions 223,995 less depreciation 61,733), rate re-measurement (Barberton 28.29%, Evander 29.03%, MTR 29.95%, Tennant 30.00% per [D006.4 p. 26]), and FCTR (+85,222 cost / +21,493 accumulated depreciation). **The explicit DTL split is not in Note 7 — still open for the deferred-tax note / D008.**

**Q48 (Low-Med): [NEW] confirm prior-period misclassification (footnotes [^1]/[^2]) changed no totals (net DTL 140,506, net DTA 2,072); check cross-references.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved`
- **Answer & evidence**: Note 7's only footnote is **[^1] "Refer to note 18"** on the acquisitions line [D006.5, p. 27] — this is a cross-reference, not a misclassification note. No footnote [^2] appears in this D006.5 capture. The PP&E roll-forward **does tie internally**: opening cost 1,082,411 + acquisitions 142,436 + additions 223,995 − disposals 2,301 + rehab 6,520 − derecognition 4,301 + transfers 2,584 + FCTR 85,222 = **1,536,566** closing cost [D006.5, p. 27]; accumulated depreciation opening (257,961) − depreciation 61,733 − impairment 345 + disposals 376 − transfers 2,661 + derecognition 3,725 − FCTR 21,493 = **(340,092)** [D006.5, p. 28]; carrying amount 1,536,566 − 340,092 = **1,196,474**, which ties to the stated carrying amount. **No total-changing misclassification evident in this section.** The deferred-tax misclassification note [^1]/[^2] from D006.4 remains to be checked in that note.

*(All other stored questions relate to sections not captured here — carried forward in Section 4.)*

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

- **Footnote [^1] — "Refer to note 18"** on the line **"Additions through acquisitions[^1] = 142,436"** [D006.5, p. 27]. This is the only footnote in the D006.5 capture. It changes interpretation of the PP&E roll-forward materially: the **142,436 acquisition addition is not organic capex** — it is the fair value of PP&E acquired via the **Emmerson business combination (Note 18)**, almost entirely **mineral rights 142,244** plus minor other (80) and right-of-use (112). Forecast implication: this is a one-off, non-cash, non-sustaining addition that must be **excluded from FY27 capex run-rate** and instead treated as part of the Emmerson acquisition balance sheet. It also explains why the **mineral rights and mining property** carrying amount jumped from 50,080 to 56,158 (owned) and "mineral rights – other" from 53,224 to 231,968 [D006.5, p. 28] — the 231,968 "other" line is dominated by the Emmerson acquisition. **Verification needed**: Note 18 (Emmerson) must confirm the 142,436 PP&E fair value, the associated deferred-tax liability, and whether any of the acquired mineral rights are subject to pending conditions. *(Footnote definition is present — not missing — but the cross-referenced Note 18 is outside this capture and must be read.)*

- **No footnote markers ([^2], *, etc.) are present on the accumulated depreciation page [D006.5, p. 28]** — no missing-footnote issue on that page.

- **Embedded qualification — "Reconciliation of depreciation and amortisation included in cost of production"** [D006.5, p. 28]. This small table is a qualification of the depreciation figure and matters for forecasting: **PP&E depreciation (61,733)** is adjusted by **(487) depreciation recognised in inventory**, **(247) amortisation of intangibles**, and **+2,179 add back: other depreciation and amortisation**, to arrive at **Total D&A in cost of production = (60,288)** vs (33,360) FY25. Implication: the **cash-cost D&A charge is 60,288**, not the 61,733 book depreciation — FY27 unit-cost modelling should use ~60,288 (or a forward run-rate scaled to the larger asset base) and not the gross depreciation line. The +2,179 "other D&A" add-back is unexplained and should be checked (likely amortisation of right-of-use/other items) — flag for D008/Note 17.

- **Derecognition line (cost (4,301); accumulated depreciation released 3,725; net NBV ~576)** [D006.5, pp. 27-28]. No footnote explains what was derecognised. Given the right-of-use asset components (buildings RoU (2,953), plant RoU (54), CWUC (717), other (577)) and the solar sale on 30 June 2026, this likely relates to **leased/solar assets retired or sold** — but the note does not say. This is a qualification gap: the FY26 P&L disposal result (gain/loss) is not derivable from Note 7 alone. **Verification needed in D005.1 / D008.**

- **Transfers line (cost 2,584; accumulated depreciation (2,661))** [D006.5, pp. 27-28]. Net transfer is slightly negative to accumulated depreciation — a small reclassification between classes (notably mineral rights −7,027 / +7,027 between "mining property" and "other", and CWUC −59,795 into plant/buildings). No forecast impact beyond confirming **CWUC of 234,145** remains under construction at 30 June 2026 — this is the forward capex pipeline to be depreciated in FY27+.

### [SECTION READING RESPONSE / DETERMINATION]
## 1. Answers to Stored Questions for This Section

Only a subset of stored questions touch the material actually visible on p. 29 (Notes 8, 9, 10, 11). No stored question is fully resolved here; several are advanced.

- **Q20 (Medium)**: Notes 4, 9, 10, 11, 12, 18, 19 — receivables (US$41,654k)/payables (US$142,294k) jump; contract liability nature; long-term inventory drawdown; Emmerson acquisition balance sheet. [D005.1 pp.17-18]
  - **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D005/D006 later notes/D008)`
  - **Answer & evidence**: Receivables jump is now substantially explained: Other receivables 41,654 = financial 7,633 (other recv 7,828 less loss allowance 195) + non-financial 34,021 (prepayments 15,914; indirect taxes 18,107) `[D006.6, p. 29]`. FY25 comparative 15,496. The prepayment increase is explicitly attributed to "prepayments made to secure cyanide supply amid the ongoing geopolitical disruptions" (footnote [^2]) — a working-capital and supply-risk item, not a revenue accrual. Long-term inventory fell 25,698→11,911, with 9,788 reclassified to current — confirming a drawdown/reclassification, not a write-down (no NRV write-down or reversal per note text). Payables composition and the Emmerson balance sheet remain open (D005.1/Note 18).

- **Q30 / Q51 (High)**: Reconcile accrual additions 223,995 to cash capex 215.8m; identify ~8.2m non-cash component. [D006.2 p.22; D006.5 p.27]
  - **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D005.1/D008)`
  - **Answer & evidence**: Note 8 confirms total capital 223,995 (FY25 167,909), split sustaining 21,832 / expansion 202,163 `[D006.6, p. 29]`. This ties the accrual-basis figure used in Note 7. The cash bridge (~8.2m non-cash) is not shown in Note 8; must be tested against the cash-flow statement (D005.1) and Note 19. Note 8 also gives the operation split needed to model FY27 capex by asset (see Section 5).

- **Q33 / Q52 (Medium)**: Solar plant sale proceeds/gain/loss; confirm Note 7 derecognition line. [D006.2 p.20; D006.5 pp.27-28]
  - **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open)`
  - **Answer & evidence**: Note 8 shows Solar projects FY26 expansion capital 7,159 (FY25 3,530) `[D006.6, p. 29]` — i.e., spend continued into FY26 before the 30 June 2026 sale. Proceeds, gain/loss and retained obligations are **not** in this section; still open.

- **Q32 / Q40 (Medium)**: Emmerson FY27 run-rate; Note 18.1 PPA/goodwill. [D006.2 pp.20,22; D006.3 p.25]
  - **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open)`
  - **Answer & evidence**: No Emmerson capex line appears in Note 8 (Tennant Mines 71,304 is the Australia capex bucket) `[D006.6, p. 29]`. Emmerson's post-acquisition capex, if any, is not separately disclosed here — flag for Note 18.1/PP&E.

- **Q9 / Q39 (High/Medium)**: Short-term investments composition; FVTPL treatment. [D005.1 p.17; D006.1 p.19; D006.4 p.25]
  - **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open)`
  - **Answer & evidence**: Note 9 shows total investments 12,791 = CuFe 12,705 + investment in associate 86 `[D006.6, p. 29]`. The CuFe stake is carried with a **FV adjustment through OCI of 1,897** (FY25: 2,107) — i.e., an FVOCI equity instrument, distinct from the Note 5 FVTPL gain of 1,142. This distinguishes two fair-value streams; OCI FV (1,897) should be excluded from earnings/EBITDA, while the 1,142 FVTPL item is in finance income (Q39 confirmation for D008 still pending). Note 13 short-term investments (137,867) is **not** the same line as Note 9 investments — confirm in D006 later notes/D008.

## 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

- **Note 8 — no footnotes.** The table is a clean accrual capex split (sustaining vs expansion). Key interpretive point: expansion capex 202,163 is 90% of total; FY27 guidance of US$302m (Q8/D008) should be tested against this base. Evander has **zero sustaining capital** (all 49,822 expansion) — verify in D008 that Evander's AISC/cash cost includes an allocated sustaining component, else unit costs are understated. Tennant expansion 68,436 (FY25 35,849) — near-doubling; check D009/D008 for what project this funds and FY27 run-rate.

- **Note 9 footnote [^1] `[D006.6, p. 29]`**: CuFe is an ASX-listed explorer/developer (Gecko/Orlando, Tennant Creek). "Tennant company acquired 307,039,759 of CuFe's issued share capital on 19 May 2026 representing a 15% shareholding." Implications: (i) the 11,265 acquisition cost is a **strategic stake**, not a consolidated asset; (ii) the 15% holding and 1,897 FVOCI movement sit outside P&L — confirm excluded from APMs (D008); (iii) the 457 FCTR movement is OCI, not tax/ETR (ties to Q46); (iv) potential future consolidation/step-up or impairment risk if CuFe re-rates — a forecast sensitivity, not a current cash item.

- **Note 10 footnote [^1] `[D006.6, p. 29]`**: "The long-term inventory relates to a holding of tailings contained in Barberton Mines' Harper TSF, Mogale Gold, MSC and Tennant Mines." Implications: this is **tailings retreatment feed inventory**; the 11,911 balance is a non-current resource that converts to current as processed. The reclassification of 9,788 to current signals near-term processing — check D009/D008 for Barberton/MTR tailings throughput and any recovery assumptions. No NRV write-down/reversal in either period (explicit note text) — supports no FY26 inventory impairment add-back.

- **Note 11 footnotes [^1], [^2], [^3] `[D006.6, p. 29]`**:
  - [^1]: Other receivables are non-operating small debtors — Evander US$4.5m (FY25 2.4m) and Barberton. Low credit risk (loss allowance 195).
  - [^2]: **"The year-on-year increase is due to prepayments made to secure cyanide supply amid the ongoing geopolitical disruptions."** This is the most consequential footnote here. It converts a 12,723 increase in prepayments into a **supply-chain hedge / working-capital build**, not a demand signal. Forecast implication: cyanide prepayment may unwind in FY27 (cash inflow) or persist if disruptions continue; test against cost-of-production (Note 3) and cash-flow (D005.1/Note 19). Also check whether prepaid cyanide is excluded from cash cost/AISC — if prepayments are treated as inventory-like, unit cost timing could distort.
  - [^3]: Indirect taxes 18,107 (FY25 8,657) = SA VAT + Australian GST. The +9,450 jump should be tested against the Australian (Tennant/Emmerson) consolidation from 22 June 2026 and SA VAT refund timing; a VAT receivable build is a cash drag, not an expense.

Share capital movement: opening 39,442; buy-back (27); closing **39,415**; par value 1 pence.

## 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

**Footnote [^1] — Note 12 Other payables** `[D006.7, physical PDF p. 30]`
- **Text**: "Other payables comprise various short-term liabilities not classified as trade payables or borrowings. These include accrued expenses for utilities and professional fees among other operational accruals. The year-on-year increase is primarily as a result of the commissioning of Tennant Mines and the processing of third-party gold-bearing material."
- **What it changes**: The +29,480 YoY increase in other payables (20,825 → 50,305) is **not** a generic working-capital build — it is explicitly tied to **Tennant commissioning** and **third-party material processing**. This means: (i) part of the payables spike is a **structural FY27 run-rate** (Tennant now commissioned, third-party processing ongoing), not a one-off; (ii) it corroborates that third-party material is a **real FY26/FY27 cost driver** (ties Q13, Q61 cyanide prepayments); (iii) it warns that the FY27 payables balance may stay elevated rather than mean-revert — important for working-capital cash-flow forecasting.
- **Verification required**: D009/D008 — confirm third-party tonnes/oz processed and the associated payables; Note 19 — confirm the cash-flow working-capital movement reflects this build; D008 — confirm whether third-party purchase costs are inside cash cost/AISC.

**Footnote [^1] — Note 14.1 Issued shares** `[D006.7, physical PDF p. 30]`
- **Text**: FY25 issues: 83,597,210 shares for Tennant acquisition; 4,298,400 for Yungatha; 24,917,607 to a former Tennant lender in settlement of debt. Total 112,813,218.
- **What it changes**: Confirms FY25 share count was inflated by **acquisition consideration and debt settlement**, not cash issuance — so FY25 EPS/dividend-per-share denominators are not a clean run-rate. The 24,917,607 debt-settlement shares also mean part of FY25 "debt" was equity-settled, relevant to FY25 finance-cost comparatives.
- **Verification required**: D005.1/D008 — confirm FY25 EPS/dividend-per-share denominators and whether any FY27 SBP/debt settlement creates further dilution.

**Footnote [^2] — Note 14.1 Share buy-back** `[D006.7, physical PDF p. 30]`
- **Text**: Buy-back during current period reduced shares by **2,003,735 at average 47.8 pence (US$0.66 cents) per share**.
- **What it changes**: Small capital return (~US$1.3m at 0.66c × 2.0m — note the "US$0.66 cents" wording is ambiguous; at 47.8p ≈ US$0.60–0.66, so ~US$1.2–1.3m). Immaterial to forecast but confirms buy-back completed; no ongoing buy-back programme disclosed here.
- **Verification required**: D005.1 — confirm buy-back cash outflow and treasury share accounting.

**Note 13 qualification (not a footnote but embedded commentary)** `[D006.7, physical PDF p. 30]`
- **Text**: "highly liquid and readily available for withdrawal. Notwithstanding their short-term nature and high liquidity, the investments do not qualify as cash and cash equivalents and are classified as financial assets at fair value through profit or loss."
- **What it changes**: This is a **deliberate accounting qualification** — management is signalling liquidity strength (relevant to going-concern/Net Cash APM) while flagging that the 137,867 sits outside "cash and cash equivalents." For forecasting: (i) the FV gain 1,142 is **P&L but non-cash** — must be stripped from EBITDA/adjusted earnings; (ii) the 137,867 is **not** in the cash-flow "cash and cash equivalents" line, so it won't appear in operating cash flow; (iii) whether it counts in "Net Cash" depends on the APM definition (D008).
- **Verification required**: D008 — Net Cash/liquidity APM reconciliation; confirm FVTPL gain excluded from adjusted earnings.

## 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

**Note 15 fn [^1] — Tennant debt-settlement shares** [D006.8, p. 31]
- *"During the previous reporting period, 24,917,607 shares were issued to a prior lender of Tennant company in settlement of an existing debt obligation."*
- **What it changes**: Confirms the FY25 share count increase was a **non-cash debt-for-equity settlement**, not a cash raise. It explains part of the FY25 share premium movement (10,877) and should be excluded from any "shares issued for cash" analysis. It also confirms the Tennant acquisition carried legacy debt that has now been extinguished (term loan settled FY26). **Verify**: no residual Tennant debt (answered above — nil); no further share issuances for debt settlement in FY27.

**Note 15 — Capital reduction (two tranches)** [D006.8, p. 31]
- *"Formal approval of the capital reduction in the current reporting period was granted by the High Court of Justice on 28 April 2025. The Court order ... was registered with the Registrar of Companies on 21 May 2026 ... the capital reduction became effective on this date. Following the share capital reduction, the Company's share premium account was cancelled in full, with the amount appropriated to retained earnings."* Also: prior-period reduction registered 18 July 2024.
- **What it changes**: This is a **presentation/equity reclassification only** (share premium → retained earnings), creating distributable reserves to support the dividend. **No P&L, cash, or EPS effect.** It explains why share premium is nil at 30 June 2026. **Verify in D008/D005.1**: confirm the appropriation amount ties to the statement of changes in equity and that distributable reserves support the FY26 final dividend (ties Q3/Q68 dividend ranking).

**Note 16.1 — RCF / JIBAR→ZARONIA transition** [D006.8, p. 31]
- *"Addenda to the relevant facility agreements were signed on 12 August 2026. The transition will take effect on the next interest rate reset/rollover date of 29 September 2026. The change is not expected to result in a substantial modification under IFRS 9."*
- **What it changes**: Post-year-end event — the RCF benchmark changes from JIBAR to ZARONIA from 29 Sep 2026. **No IFRS 9 modification gain/loss**, so no one-off P&L. But **FY27 finance cost modelling must use ZARONIA-based pricing**, not JIBAR — flag for the forecast. Also confirms the RCF remains live into FY27 (maturity extended to 30 June 2027).

**Note 16.3 — DMTN / JIBAR→ZARONIA transition** [D006.8, p. 32]
- *"The Group will proceed with amending and restating its applicable pricing supplements applicable to the DMTN bonds once legislation has been enacted for PARS02 and PARS03 regarding the transition from JIBAR to ZARONIA. The change is not expected to result in a substantial modification under IFRS 9."*
- **What it changes**: PARS01 fully settled; PARS03 partially settled; **PARS02 and PARS03 remain outstanding** (balance 51,475). The benchmark transition is pending legislation — a **FY27+ event**. No modification gain/loss expected. **Verify in D008**: DMTN covenant terms and the renewable-energy KPI coupon step-up (Q5) are **not disclosed in this capture** — this is a material gap for FY27 finance cost modelling (a KPI-linked coupon step-up could raise the effective DMTN rate).

**Note 16.7 fn [^1] — Guarantees** [D006.8, p. 33]
- *"The guarantees issued to Eskom Holdings SOC Limited relate to the supply of electricity. RMB issued a guarantee to Eskom on behalf of MTR resulting in an increase in the Eskom guarantee. The guarantees issued to the DMPR relate to the Group's environmental rehabilitation obligation."*
- **What it changes**: The DMPR guarantee of US$40,575k (up from 37,626) is tied to the **environmental rehabilitation obligation** — this is a contingent/restricted item, not debt, and should **not** be deducted in Net Cash APM. Eskom guarantee US$5,271k (up from 3,761) reflects MTR electricity supply — an operating contingency. **Verify**: whether the DMPR guarantee is backed by restricted cash/rehab fund (ties Q22) and whether it constrains liquidity.

**Note 16.7 fn [^2] — General banking facility** [D006.8, p. 33]
- *"The Nedbank Limited and RMB general banking facilities are unsecured and were unutilised in the current and previous reporting periods. These facilities, when utilised, bear interest at rates linked to the South African prime interest rate."*
- **What it changes**: The US$18,226k general banking facility is **unutilised** — genuine undrawn headroom, relevant to the US$78.9m "immediately available" liquidity reconciliation (Q9/Q25). Prime-linked when drawn.

**Note 16.7 fn [^3] — US$ gold and derivative trading facilities** [D006.8, p. 33]
- *"The US$ gold and derivative trading facilities are used by the Group for the purpose of trading gold inventory and subsequent conversion of US$ sales proceeds into ZAR. The facilities are held at Absa, Nedbank, RMB and Investec."*
- **What it changes**: These US$58,931k trading facilities (up sharply from 35,003) and the gold loan facility US$25,557k (up from 16,338) are **operational/trading, not borrowings** — they should be excluded from Net Cash APM debt. The gold loan facility is a working-capital inventory financing tool; its increase may signal higher inventory levels or hedging activity. **Verify**: whether gold loan drawings create an off-balance-sheet obligation or are already reflected in inventory/payables; confirm excluded from Net Cash APM (ties Q5/Q70).
