18. ACQUISITIONS AND DISPOSALS
18.1 Acquisition of Emmerson Resources Limited (Emmerson)
The Group, through its wholly owned subsidiary Tennant company, acquired 100% of the issued shares 
in Emmerson by way of an Australian Court-approved scheme of arrangement ( scheme) in accordance 
with Part 5.1 of the Australian Corporations Act 2001. Tennant company obtained control of Emmerson 
on 22 June 2026 as the scheme came into effect and was legally enforceable and binding on all parties on 
22 June 2026, as determined by section 411(10) of the Corporations Act 2001.
The acquisition represents Pan African’s next step since acquiring Tennant company in FY25 and consolidates 
its position in the TCMF. In addition, Emmerson has been a joint venture partner since establishing the Tennant 
Creek Joint Venture in 2020. Bringing the assets under single ownership allows Pan African to optimise project 
sequencing and capital allocation across the region, effectively facilitating economies of scale by eliminating the 
complexity of joint venture arrangements, and positions Pan African to capture the full value of Tennant Creek 
through consolidation, ultimately maximising value for all shareholders. 
Under the terms of the scheme, Emmerson shareholders were entitled to receive 0.1493 new Pan African shares, 
in the form of ASX-listed Pan African CDIs, for each Emmerson share held. The scheme was implemented and 
Pan African shares were allotted to the Emmerson shareholders on 1 July 2026.
Furthermore, in conjunction with the scheme, Pan African listed on the ASX by way of a Foreign Exempt Listing, 
providing Emmerson shareholders with the ability to trade Pan African CDIs on the ASX. Pan African’s shares 
continue to trade, as a dual primary issuer, on the LSE and JSE following the ASX listing.
The purchase price, determined by way of a fixed exchange rate, was settled on 1 July 2026. 
102,641,421 Pan African shares were issued at GBP1.09 (US$1.44) per share resulting in a purchase price of 
US$148.2 million. As control arose on 22 June 2026 and the purchase price settled on 1 July 2026, being after 
the reporting date, a reserve for shares to be issued was recognised as at 30 June 2026; refer to note 14.
While Tennant company obtained control of Emmerson on 22 June 2026, for practical purposes and given that 
there were no significant transactions or movements in fair values between 22 June 2026 and 30 June 2026, 
Emmerson has been consolidated from 30 June 2026.
Included in the identifiable assets acquired are inputs in the form of mineral rights. However, no active operations, 
assets or a skilled workforce to extract gold or generate income from ordinary activities were identified on the 
date of acquisition. The acquisition of Emmerson, therefore, did not constitute the acquisition of a business as 
there was no integrated set of activities in place capable of being managed to convert the acquired inputs into 
outputs. As such, the acquisition was recognised as an asset acquisition.
 On acquisition, Tennant company was required to identify and recognise the individual assets and liabilities 
acquired. The purchase price was allocated to the assets acquired and liabilities assumed based on their fair 
values at the date of acquisition, resulting in the excess purchase consideration over net identifiable asset 
value being allocated to the exploration assets. This allocation reflects the concentration of value in the mineral 
extraction and exploration rights acquired. Since the transaction did not constitute the acquisition of a business, 
no goodwill was recognised

The current portion of US$43,969k is the balance sheet liability due within 12 months — it is NOT the FY27 cash outflow forecast; actual cash settlement will depend on vesting and share price at settlement. The scheme split shows Pan African Share Appreciation Bonus Plan 20,555 (FY25: 5,553) and PAR Gold LTIP 36,717 (FY25: 11,408); total P&L 57,272 vs the FY25 comparative total of 16,961 (note: FY25 total in the reconciliation is 13,358 + 6,325 capitalised = 19,683, which does not tie to 16,961 — see Q83 below). **Still open**: FY27 expected cash settlement (vs balance-sheet current portion); split of the 1,463 capitalised vs 57,272 expensed for AISC/EBITDA treatment (see Q84); the apparent FY25 arithmetic tension (Q83).

## 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

**Note 17.1 — Cash-settled SBP reconciliation and scheme split** `[D006.9, physical PDF p. 33]`
- The reconciliation shows FY26 expense 57,272, capitalised 1,463, payments (18,200), FX 2,331, closing 64,352, current 43,969, non-current 20,383.
- **Arithmetic tension (flag immediately)**: The scheme table shows total P&L expense 57,272 with FY25 comparative 16,961 (20,555 + 36,717 = 57,272 for FY26; 5,553 + 11,408 = 16,961 for FY25). But the reconciliation shows FY25 expense recognised in P&L of 13,358 plus capitalised 6,325 = 19,683, which does not equal 16,961. The difference is 2,722. Either the FY25 scheme table excludes something or the reconciliation comparative is inconsistent — must be verified (Q83).
- **What it changes**: The current portion 43,969 is a balance-sheet liability, not a cash forecast. Actual FY27 cash settlement depends on vesting (90% probability per Note 17.3) and share price at settlement (90-day VWAP 30.69 at 30 June 2026 vs grant-date prices 3.59–10.43). The PAR Gold LTIP expense of 36,717 is the dominant driver (FY25: 11,408), reflecting the share price re-rating from ~ZAR10.40 to ~ZAR30.69 — a mark-to-market swing that is non-cash and should be excluded from Adjusted EBITDA but is a real future cash obligation when vested.
- **Verification needed**: D008 must confirm the SBP MTM exclusion from Adjusted EBITDA and the FY27 expected cash settlement.

**Note 17.2 — Assumptions and estimates** `[D006.9, physical PDF p. 33]`
- Weighted average share price ZAR15.17 (FY25: 3.92); exercise/strike ZAR5.21 (FY25: 3.81); exercise price range ZAR3.19–20.95 (FY25: 1.36–8.05); volatility 45.6–51.9% (FY25: 42–44%); expected life 3–6 years; weighted average remaining life 3.41 years (FY25: 3.88); risk-free rate 7.49–7.60% (FY25: 7.15–7.57%); dividend yield 3% (FY25: 3%).
- **What it changes**: The higher share price and volatility inputs materially increased fair value and thus the FY26 expense. The 3.41-year weighted remaining life implies the 64,352 liability unwinds over ~3–4 years, not all in FY27 — relevant to the current/non-current split and FY27 cash forecast.

**Note 17.3 — PAR Gold LTIP Monte Carlo inputs** `[D006.9, physical PDF p. 34]`
- G shares: 11,541,455; grant 1 July 2023; vest 30 June 2026; grant VWAP ZAR3.59; 30 June 2026 VWAP ZAR30.69; 30 June 2025 VWAP ZAR10.40; vesting probability 90%; FV per option 30 June 2026 ZAR8.27 (unchanged from 30 June 2025 ZAR8.27).
- I shares: 8,871,240; grant 1 July 2024; vest 30 June 2027; grant VWAP ZAR5.51; FV per option ZAR30.85 (30 June 2025: ZAR9.36) — a 3.3× increase.
- J shares: 7,066,577; grant 1 July 2025; vest 30 June 2028; grant VWAP ZAR10.43; FV per option ZAR28.07.
- **What it changes**: G shares vest 30 June 2026 — the 43,969 current portion likely relates largely to G shares settling in FY27. I shares vest 30 June 2027 (FY27 expense continues). J shares vest 30 June 2028. The 90% vesting probability is an assumption; a share-price fall would reduce the liability and expense (MTM), a rise would increase it. **Verification**: D008 should confirm the FY27 cash settlement expectation for G shares and the split of the 43,969 current portion.

**Note 18.1 — Emmerson acquisition (asset acquisition, not business combination)** `[D006.9, physical PDF p. 34]`
- Key qualification: "no active operations, assets or a skilled workforce to extract gold or generate income from ordinary activities were identified on the date of acquisition... did not constitute the acquisition of a business... recognised as an asset acquisition... no goodwill was recognised."
- **What it changes**: This is critical for forecasting — Emmerson contributes NO FY26 revenue (consolidated from 30 June 2026, no significant transactions between 22 and 30 June) and is an exploration/development asset, not a producing mine. The 142,244 exploration asset will not generate revenue until developed; FY27 P&L impact is likely exploration expense/capitalised development, not production cost. This corrects any assumption that Emmerson adds FY27 production ounces.
- **Purchase price allocation**: Total cost 160,097 = shares 148,218 + transaction costs 11,879. Net assets acquired 160,097: PP&E 142,436 (exploration 142,244, leased buildings 112, other 80), investment 86, receivables 6,427, cash 12,796, lease liability (133), payables (1,515). Note the cash acquired of 12,796 — relevant to the Net Cash APM and FY27 opening cash (Q5/Q76/Q77).
- **Transaction costs capitalised**: 11,879 capitalised to the acquisition, NOT expensed — so they should not be in Corporate other expenses 52,443 (Q28). This is a key reconciliation point for D008.
- **Verification needed**: Confirm in D006.5/D008 the DTL matching the 142,244 exploration asset (Q43/Q50); confirm the 12,796 cash acquired is included in the 30 June 2026 cash balance and in Net Cash APM; confirm no FY27 Emmerson revenue/tonnes in D009.

**Note 18.2 — Disposals** `[D006.9, physical PDF p. 34]`
- "There were no disposals during the current or previous reporting period." This confirms the Solar sale (Q33/Q52/Q64) was not a Note 18 disposal — it must be a PP&E derecognition/disposal within Note 7, not an acquisition/disposal note item. **Verification**: confirm in D006.5/D008 the Solar sale treatment and proceeds (Q52).

**Missing footnote check**: No footnote markers (`[^1]`, `*`) with missing definitions were identified in this capture. The tables are self-contained with no footnote references. No fabrication required.

  20.2 Fair value information                          
     Fair value of financial instruments               
     The directors consider the carrying amounts of financial assets and liabilities to approximate their fair values due
     to their short-term nature.                       
     Fair value hierarchy                              
     Financial instruments measured at fair value are classified in the fair value hierarchy based on the extent to which
     fair value is observable.                         
     The levels are determined as follows:             
     Level 1 – Fair value is based on quoted prices in active markets for identical financial assets or liabilities.
     Level 2 – Fair value is determined using inputs other than quoted prices included within Level 1 that are
         observable for the asset or liability, either directly (i.e. prices) or indirectly (i.e. derived from prices).
     Level 3 – Fair value is determined on inputs not based on observable market data.

Power purchase agreement
The Company entered into a PPA on 3 July 2025 with NOA Group Trading Proprietary Limited. The PPA is for
the supply of wheeled power for 10 years, with the option to extend it for another five years. The underlying solar
plant remains under construction with various supply agreements remaining outstanding for the plant to become
operational. The accounting treatment of recognising the PPA as an executory contract is unchanged from the
prior reporting period.
Contingent liabilities
The Group identified no material contingent liabilities in the current or previous reporting period.

**Q5 (High) — Net Cash APM reconciliation: which debt/financial liability items are deducted in US$185.7m**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D008)`
- **Answer & evidence**: Note 20.1 [D006.10, physical PDF p. 35] confirms the balance-sheet split that any Net Cash APM must reconcile to:
  - Borrowings **51,535** (FY25: 189,977) — at amortised cost
  - Trade and other payables **126,369** (FY25: 64,837) — at amortised cost
  - Financial liabilities **1,239** (FY25: 3,306)
  - Derivative financial liability **nil** FY26 (FY25: 1,848)
  - Cash and cash equivalents **108,312**; restricted cash **2,612**; short-term investments **137,867** (FVTPL, Level 2); environmental rehab fund **34,617** (FVTPL, Level 2); investment (CuFe) **12,705** (FVOCI, Level 1).
  **Arithmetic tension flagged**: 108,312 + 2,612 + 137,867 = 248,791 of cash/liquid assets vs borrowings 51,535 → gross surplus ~197,256; a US$185.7m Net Cash figure implies ~11,556 of deductions (or exclusion of restricted cash 2,612 plus ~8,944 of other items). The exact APM deduction list (whether payables 126,369, financial liabilities 1,239, leases, or restricted cash are netted) is **not disclosed in Note 20** and must be confirmed in D008. DMTN covenants/KPI step-up also still open (Q75/Q77/Q87).

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

**Note 20.2, fn 1 — CuFe Level 1** [D006.10, p. 36]
- "The investment in CuFe is included as Level 1 as the shares are traded on the ASX."
- **Effect**: CuFe 12,705 is a quoted, liquid asset — relevant to APM/Net Cash treatment (is it included in "immediately available" liquidity?) and to the FVOCI reserve. Must verify in D008 whether CuFe is counted in Net Cash or excluded as a strategic stake (Q60).

**Note 20.2, fn 2 — Environmental rehabilitation obligation fund Level 2** [D006.10, p. 36]
- "…premiums are invested in interest-bearing short-term deposits and equity share portfolios held in an insurance investment product managed by independent fund managers."
- **Effect**: The 34,617 rehab fund is **not** plain cash — it holds equity portfolios, so its fair value can move with markets (FY26 FV gain 3,029 per Note 19). It is a restricted/earmarked asset backing rehabilitation obligations (DMPR guarantee 39,566). Forecast implication: do **not** treat 34,617 as free liquidity; it should be excluded from Net Cash or netted against rehab obligations. Confirm D008 treatment (Q5/Q80).

**Note 20.2, fn 3 — Short-term investments Level 2** [D006.10, p. 36]
- "…valued using the net asset value per unit, which is derived from observable market inputs for the underlying money market and short-term instruments held by each fund."
- **Effect**: The 137,867 is money-market/short-term instrument exposure valued at NAV — genuinely liquid (supports inclusion in "immediately available" liquidity / Net Cash APM), but not cash equivalents for IFRS cash-flow purposes. The FV gain 1,142 (Note 19) flows through P&L and is added back as non-cash; confirm APM exclusion in D008 (Q67/Q39).

**Note 21 — Power purchase agreement qualification** [D006.10, p. 36]
- "The PPA is for the supply of wheeled power for 10 years, with the option to extend it for another five years. The underlying solar plant remains under construction with various supply agreements remaining outstanding for the plant to become operational. The accounting treatment of recognising the PPA as an executory contract is unchanged from the prior reporting period."
- **Effect**: This is the most consequential forward-looking qualification in the section. (1) The solar plant is **not yet operational**, so FY27 power-cost benefits are deferred until commissioning — do not model PPA cost savings in FY27 until the plant is operational. (2) The PPA is an **executory contract**, i.e. off-balance-sheet — no lease/financial liability recognised, but a real 10–15-year power cost commitment that will hit cash costs once operational. (3) "Various supply agreements remaining outstanding" links to the 94,580 open orders / 309,076 uncontracted commitments. Must verify in D008/D009: solar plant commissioning date, expected PPA tariff vs grid/Eskom cost, and whether the 309,076 uncontracted commitments include solar balance-of-plant.

**Note 21 — "No material contingent liabilities"** [D006.10, p. 36]
- **Effect**: Contradicts/qualifies the previously captured ~US$3.0m Barberton groundwater NEMA matter (Q22) — either provided for (in rehab/environmental provision) or judged not material. Must reconcile to Note 16/Note 18 rehab provision and confirm no unrecorded exposure. Also note DMPR guarantees **39,566** here vs **40,575** previously captured from Note 16 — a **~1,009 difference** to reconcile (presentation/rounding/timing).

**Note 20.2 — Fair value hierarchy: no Level 3 instruments** [D006.10, p. 36]
- FY26 and FY25 both show only Level 1 and Level 2 instruments; the FY25 derivative liability (1,848) was Level 2. **Effect**: no unobservable-marking risk; the FY25 derivative was settled in FY26 (Note 19 shows settlement of derivative (1,873)). Confirms clean, observable fair values.

**Note 19 — finance costs 17,318 vs Note 5 expensed borrowing cost 14,505** [D006.10, p. 35]
- **Effect**: A ~2,813 gap exists between the cash-flow reconciliation finance cost (17,318) and the Note 5 borrowing cost (14,505). This is likely lease interest + unwinding of rehabilitation/provision discount, but must be reconciled — it affects the FY27 finance-cost run-rate (base = DMTN + RCF interest plus non-borrowing finance costs).

**Note 19 — "Adjusted for: 143,482" subtotal arithmetic** [D006.10, p. 35]
- Check: 57,272 − 4,706 + 17,318 + 781 + 14,609 + 163 − 109 − 3,029 − 1,142 + 61,980 + 345 = **143,482** ✓ (bargain purchase nil, derivative nil, contract liability nil FY26). Arithmetic ties.

--- Section D006.11 | original physical page 36 | text selection | Mixed / unspecified ---
22. RELATED PARTY TRANSACTIONS
The related party transactions are summarised as follows:
• Intra-Group interest and management fees – refer to segment analysis note 3
• Intra-Group loans have no specific repayment terms, are repayable on demand and bear interest in relation 
to the treasury function provided by Funding Company
• Intra-Group PAR Gold reciprocal dividend – refer to the summarised consolidated statement of changes 
in equity
• Intra-Group electricity charge between Evander Solar Solutions Proprietary Limited and Evander Mines for 
the electricity produced by the solar plant and utilised by Elikhulu – refer to segment analysis note 3.
No further material related party transactions occurred, either with third parties or with Group entities, during 
the current or previous reporting period.
23. LITIGATION AND CLAIMS
Evander Mines and MPC
Evander Mines terminated the contract mining agreement (CMA) with its 8 Shaft contractor during the 2024 
reporting period due to disputes over specific clauses in the CMA. Evander Mines referred this matter to 
arbitration and the proceedings are still ongoing. The likelihood of any outflow of economic benefits is remote.
	 Department of Forestry, Fisheries and the Environment – alleged offences in the 
Barberton Nature Reserve
On 22 May 2025, the South African state served a summons on Barberton Mines and its environmental health 
and safety manager for alleged contraventions of the National Environmental Management: Protected Areas 
Act, 57 of 2003, and related regulations. The charges relate to (i) conducting commercial prospecting in a 
nature reserve and (ii) the unauthorised widening and upgrading of a road within a nature reserve. Barberton 
Mines denies the merits of the charges and submitted formal written representations to the National Prosecuting 
Authority (NPA) on 17 September 2025, requesting the NPA to, inter alia, withdraw the charges. The NPA is still 
considering the representation and has been directed by the Court to provide Barberton Mines with a response 
on the representation by 29 September 2026. The likelihood of any outflow of economic benefits is remote.
Barberton Mines land claim
Barberton Mines is aware of a land claim, lodged by individuals purporting to be part of communities surrounding 
Barberton Mines’ Sheba Mine, pertaining to two portions of land, over which Barberton Mines holds two 
converted mining rights. Barberton Mines is formally opposing the land claim and has reasonable prospects to 
successfully challenge the claim. The Group’s legal counsel has advised that, irrespective of the merits of the 
claim, there should be no impact on the Company’s ability to exercise its mining right and continue operations.
24. EVENTS AFTER THE REPORTING PERIOD
As announced on the SENS, on 1 July 2026, the Company issued 102,641,421 new Pan African shares (in the 
form of ASX-listed Pan African CDIs) to eligible Emmerson shareholders in settlement for the acquisition of 100% 
of the issued shares in Emmerson.
There were no other events that could have a material impact on the financial results of the Group after 
30 June 2026 up to the date on which the Group financial statements for the reporting period ended 
30 June 2026 were authorised for issue

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

- **No footnote markers (`[^1]`, `*`) appear in the supplied D006.11 text.** No missing-footnote issue arises for this capture; nothing to reconstruct.
- **Note 22 (Related party)** [D006.11, p. 36] — qualifications that change interpretation:
  - *"Intra-Group loans have no specific repayment terms, are repayable on demand and bear interest in relation to the treasury function provided by Funding Company."* Implication: intra-group balances are **current/demand** in nature (supports the Funding Company split flagged in Q31 — the 11,184/4,891 split ex-intra-group remains to be confirmed); no fixed maturity to model.
  - *"Intra-Group PAR Gold reciprocal dividend – refer to statement of changes in equity."* Implication: a **non-cash equity movement**, not P&L — must be excluded from forecast earnings and from any dividend cash forecast.
  - *"Intra-Group electricity charge between Evander Solar Solutions and Evander Mines for electricity produced by the solar plant and utilised by Elikhulu – refer to segment analysis note 3."* Implication: the solar plant is **producing and being utilised** — this is a **post-period run-rate signal** that sits in tension with the earlier Note 21 finding that the PPA is executory and the plant "not operational." **Verification required**: confirm in D008 whether solar opex/PPA costs are excluded from AISC and whether the intra-group electricity charge is eliminated on consolidation (no group-level revenue/cost effect), and in D009 whether Elikhulu throughput reflects solar supply.
- **Note 23 (Litigation)** [D006.11, p. 36] — all three matters state likelihood of outflow **remote**; consistent with Note 21 "no material contingent liabilities." Qualification to test: "remote" is a legal-probability judgement, not a quantified exposure — check D008 for any disclosed contingent liability amounts and whether the Barberton NEM:PAA matter (NPA response due 29 Sep 2026) is disclosed as a post-balance-sheet contingency.
- **Note 24 (Events after reporting period)** [D006.11, p. 36] — the Emmerson share issue is dated **1 July 2026**, one day after year-end; the "no other events" wording is a **negative assurance** only up to the authorisation date. Verification: confirm in D008/D009 the exact authorisation date and whether any July 2026 trading/run-rate update is given elsewhere (ties to Q37 July 2026 delivery vs budget).

### [FORENSIC EXTRACTION: CAPTURED TEXT & FINANCIAL TABLES]
--- Section D008.1 | original physical page 38 | text selection | Commentary ---
INTRODUCTION
When assessing and discussing Pan African’s reported financial performance, financial position and cash flows, 
management makes reference to alternative performance measures (APMs) of historical or future financial performance, 
financial position or cash flows that are not defined or specified under IFRS Accounting Standards.
The APMs include financial APMs, non-financial APMs and ratios, as described below.
• Financial APMs: These financial measures are usually derived from the annual financial statements which have been 
prepared in accordance with IFRS Accounting Standards. Certain financial measures cannot be directly derived from 
the annual financial statements as they contain additional information such as financial information from earlier periods 
or profit estimates or projections. The accounting policies applied when calculating APMs are, where relevant and 
unless otherwise stated, the same as those disclosed in the consolidated financial statements for the year ended 
30 June 2026.
• Non-financial APMs: These measures incorporate certain non-financial information that management believes is useful 
when assessing the performance of the Group.
• Ratios: Ratios may be calculated using any of the APMs referred to above, IFRS Accounting Standards measures or 
a combination of APMs and IFRS Accounting Standards measures. APMs are not uniformly defined by all companies 
and may not be comparable with APM disclosures made by other companies, and they exclude:
– measures defined or specified by an applicable reporting framework such as revenue, profit or loss or earnings 
per share
– physical or non-financial measures such as number of employees, number of subscribers, revenue per unit 
measure (when the revenue figures are extracted directly from the annual financial statements) or social and 
environmental measures such as gas emissions, breakdown of workforce by contract or geographical location
– information on major shareholdings, acquisition or disposal of own shares and total number of voting rights
– information to explain the compliance with the terms of an agreement or legislative requirements such as lending 
covenants or the basis of calculating director or executive remuneration.
APMs should be considered in addition to, and not as a substitute for or as superior to, measures of financial 
performance, financial position or cash flows reported in accordance with IFRS Accounting Standards.
PURPOSE OF APMs
The Group uses APMs to improve the comparability of information between reporting periods and reporting segments by 
adjusting for uncontrollable or once-off factors which impact IFRS Accounting Standards measurements and disclosures 
to aid the user of the integrated annual report in understanding the activity taking place across the Group’s portfolio. 
The directors are responsible for preparing the APMs.
Their use is driven by characteristics particularly visible in the mining sector.
• Earnings volatility: The sector is characterised by significant volatility in earnings driven by movements in 
macroeconomic factors, primarily commodity prices and foreign exchange rates. This volatility is outside the 
control of management and can mask underlying changes in performance. As such, when comparing year-on-year 
performance, management excludes certain non-recurring items to aid comparability and then quantifies and isolates 
uncontrollable factors to improve understanding of the controllable portion of variances.
• Nature of investment: Investments in the sector are typically capital-intensive and occur over several years requiring 
significant funding before generating cash. These investments are often made through debt and equity providers, and 
the nature of the Group’s ownership interest affects how the financial results of these operations are reflected in the 
Group’s results, for example, whether full consolidation (subsidiaries), consolidation of the Group’s attributable assets 
and liabilities (joint operations) or equity-accounted (associates and joint ventures).
• Portfolio complexity: At year-end, the Group’s operating portfolio remains largely in commodities, mainly gold, which 
accounts for 99.7% of the Group’s revenue. The cost, value of and return from each saleable unit (such as tonne or 
ounce) therefore do not differ materially between each operating business. This makes understanding both the overall 
portfolio performance and the relative performance of each mining operation on a like-for-like basis less challenging.
Consequently, APMs are used by the board and management for planning and reporting. A subset is also used by 
management in setting director and management remuneration. The measures are also used in discussions with the 
investment analyst community and credit rating agencies.

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

No footnote markers (`[^1]`, `*`) appear in the supplied D008.1 text or tables. However, several **embedded qualifications** materially change interpretation:

1. **"Excluding non-gold operations" in sustaining/development capital (AISC)** `[D008.1, p. 38]`. This means the AISC numerator excludes sustaining capital on non-gold assets (e.g., the agricultural/CWUC operations, Tennant's non-gold by-products). Implication: Group AISC US$1,867/oz is not a full cash-cost of the group; non-gold capex sits outside AISC and must be forecast separately. Verify in the printed AISC reconciliation (pp. 40–47) whether any non-gold capex is excluded and its quantum.

2. **"The value of by-product revenue is deducted from operating costs as it effectively reduces the cost of gold production"** `[D008.1, p. 39]`. This is the by-product credit mechanic — material for Tennant (and any base-metal credits). Forecast implication: AISC is sensitive to by-product price decks (copper, etc.), not just gold. Verify the by-product credit quantum and price assumptions in the AISC reconciliation and D009.

3. **Adjusted EBITDA exclusion list is narrower than expected** `[D008.1, pp. 38–39]`. The only exclusions are tax, D&A, net finance costs, impairment/reversals, bargain purchase gain, and unrealised FV gains/losses on derivatives in the normal course. **SBP expense, restructuring, transaction costs, and FX are NOT listed.** This contradicts the stored hypothesis (Q84) that SBP MTM sits in a Corporate exclusion — if the printed reconciliation shows a 52,443 "Corporate/other" exclusion, it must be characterised differently (e.g., as part of "non-recurring items" under the generic "Excludes the impact of non-recurring items" rationale, which is looser than the enumerated list). Verification required against the printed reconciliation.

4. **"EBITDA" and "Adjusted EBITDA" share the same enumerated adjustments** `[D008.1, pp. 38–39]` — the only difference is the rationale wording ("non-recurring items" vs the enumerated list). This is internally inconsistent: the table gives EBITDA and Adjusted EBITDA identical adjustment columns. Verify which line items actually differ in the printed reconciliation (Q36).

5. **Net debt definition adds "financial liabilities" and "restricted cash"** `[D008.1, p. 39]`. Restricted cash 2,612 is added back (i.e., excluded from cash). Financial liabilities 1,239 are added. This partly explains the ~11,556 gap (Q5) but not fully — the remaining gap likely includes IFRS 9 adjustments and lease liabilities (IFRS 16). Verify the printed Net Cash reconciliation.

6. **"Adjusted for non-cash flow items as determined in accordance with IAS 7" in Free cash flow** `[D008.1, p. 39]` — this is a catch-all that could include SBP, unrealised FX, and provisions. Verify the printed FCF reconciliation for the specific add-backs.

7. **"Less capital expenditure funded through permitted indebtedness" in Free cash flow** `[D008.1, p. 39]` — this is an unusual adjustment: capex funded by debt is deducted from FCF, which effectively double-counts debt-funded capex (once as capex, once as debt service). Verify the quantum and whether this is used in any covenant KPI (Q92).

   ALL-IN COSTS                                            
   This includes additional costs which relate to the growth of the Group. AIC starts with AISC and adds additional costs
   which relate to the growth of the Group, including non-sustaining capital expenditure not associated with current
   operations and costs such as voluntary severance pay.   
   AISC and AIC are reported on the basis of a ZAR per kilogramme of gold1 and US$ per ounce of gold. The US$
   equivalent is converted at the average exchange rate applicable for the current reporting period as disclosed in the
   Group’s operational production table on pages 92 to 97. A kilogramme of gold is converted to an ounce of gold at
   a ratio of 1:32.1509.                                   
   The following tables set out a reconciliation of Pan African’s cost of production as calculated in accordance with
   IFRS Accounting Standards to AISC and AIC for the financial years ended 30 June 2026 and 30 June 2025.
   The equivalent of the ZAR per kilogramme1 and US$ per ounce basis is disclosed in the Group’s operational
   production table on pages 92 to 97.                     
   1 A$ per kilogramme of gold.

### [SECTION READING RESPONSE / DETERMINATION]
## 1. Answers to Stored Questions for This Section

**Q1 (High) — Five-mine AISC table; FY27 AISC step-up to US$2,075-2,175/oz bridge; price/FX deck.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved` (FY26/FY25 five-mine AISC table located and captured; FY27 step-up bridge and price/FX deck remain open)
- **Answer & evidence**: The five-mine AISC table is present in D008.2. FY26 US$ AISC/oz: Barberton 2,589; Evander 1,914; BTRP 1,564; Elikhulu 1,231; MTR 1,389; Tennant 2,261; **Group total 1,867**. FY25 comparatives: Barberton 2,065; Evander 2,110; BTRP 971; Elikhulu 1,077; MTR 1,282; Tennant 1,637; **Group 1,600** `[D008.2, physical PDF p. 42; p. 43]`. FX decks: FY26 US$/ZAR 16.90, US$/A$ 0.68; FY25 US$/ZAR 18.17, US$/A$ 0.65 `[D008.2, pp. 42–43]`. **Still open**: the FY27 AISC guidance of US$2,075-2,175/oz and the bridge/price-FX assumptions behind it are not in these pages — need D008 pp. 44–47 or D002/D003.

**Q2 (High) — Exact Sheba (~17,033oz) and Consort (~7,287oz) unit costs; residual ~834oz higher-cost bucket.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved` (mine-level AISC now available; Sheba/Consort sub-mine split still not disclosed)
- **Answer & evidence**: D008.2 gives mine-level (not shaft-level) AISC only. Barberton FY26 AISC US$2,589/oz on 70,086oz sold; Evander US$1,914/oz on 47,112oz; Tennant US$2,261/oz on 32,124oz `[D008.2, p. 42]`. The D008.1 higher-cost bucket (US$3,487/oz on 25,154oz) is **not reconcilable** to any single mine here — Barberton at 2,589 is the highest mine-level AISC but still below 3,487. The ~834oz residual and Sheba/Consort sub-split remain open for D009 or D008 pp. 44–47.

**Q5 / Q90 / Q91 / Q115 (High) — Net Cash APM reconciliation; deduction list; ~11,556 gap vs US$185.7m; ST investments 137,867.**
- **Status**: `Unresolved — not addressed in this section`
- **Answer & evidence**: D008.2 covers AISC/AIC only; no net debt/cash reconciliation appears on pp. 40–43. Carry forward to D008 pp. 44–47 or D006 financial notes.

**Q8 (Low-Med) — AIC = AISC + expansion capital + once-off non-production costs; reconcile expansion capital to cash capex bridge.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved`
- **Answer & evidence**: AIC definition confirmed `[D008.2, p. 40]`: AIC starts with AISC and adds non-sustaining capex not associated with current operations plus costs such as voluntary severance. FY26 Group AISC US$508.4m + expansion capital US$195.0m = AIC US$703.4m `[D008.2, p. 40]`. FY25: AISC 315.2 + VSP 1.4 + expansion 152.7 = AIC 469.3 `[D008.2, p. 41]`. Note FY26 has **no** VSP line (nil), so AIC = AISC + expansion only. **Still open**: reconciliation of the US$195.0m expansion capital to the cash capex bridge (Q58/Q122).

**Q9 (High) — ST investments 137,867 APM inclusion; US$78.9m "immediately available".**
- **Status**: `Unresolved — not addressed in this section`
- **Answer & evidence**: No balance-sheet/net-cash content on pp. 40–43. Carry forward.

**Q11 / Q27 / Q28 / Q83 / Q84 / Q85 / Q87 / Q102 / Q111 (High) — SBP treatment in Adjusted EBITDA; Corporate 52,443 vs SBP 57,272.**
- **Status**: `Unresolved — not addressed in this section`
- **Answer & evidence**: D008.2 is AISC/AIC only; no Adjusted EBITDA reconciliation here. Note however that SBP is **not** added back in the AISC/AIC reconciliation — AISC is built from IFRS gold cost of production, not Adjusted EBITDA, so this section does not resolve the Adjusted EBITDA treatment. Carry forward to D008 pp. 44–47.

**Q22 / Q80 / Q96 / Q97 / Q105 (High) — DMPR guarantee, rehab backing, Barberton groundwater, litigation.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (indirect)`
- **Answer & evidence**: The AISC table includes a line "Reclamation and remediation – accretion and amortisation (operating sites)" of **(0.6)** FY26 and **(0.5)** FY25 `[D008.2, pp. 40–41]` — a credit, i.e. accretion/amortisation is deducted in arriving at AISC. This confirms only the operating-site accretion charge flows through AISC; it says nothing about the Barberton groundwater provision (~US$3.0m / US$0.7m discounted) or the DMPR guarantee 39,566 vs Note 16 40,575. Those remain open.

**Q23 / Q100 (Medium) — Impairment 345; price/discount-rate assumptions.**
- **Status**: `Unresolved — not addressed in this section`
- **Answer & evidence**: No impairment or price-deck content on pp. 40–43. The US$/ZAR 16.90 and US$/A$ 0.68 are **average realised/translation rates**, not the impairment price deck. Carry forward.

**Q26 / Q101 (Low-Med) — TSF capex/closure split; IFRS16 leases in net debt; lease commitments 3,214 / fin liab 418.**
- **Status**: `Unresolved — not addressed in this section`
- **Answer & evidence**: AISC includes "Sustaining capital – maintenance" and "– development" but does not split TSF closure vs capex. Lease quantum not shown. Carry forward.

**Q31 / Q38 / Q95 (High) — Finance costs 17,318 vs 14,505 (~2,813 gap); borrowing cost base.**
- **Status**: `Unresolved — not addressed in this section`
- **Answer & evidence**: Finance costs are not an AISC/AIC line (AISC uses cash cost of production, not net finance costs). Carry forward.

## 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

**Footnote 1 on p. 40 (AIC definition page):** "*A$ per kilogramme of gold.*" — This footnote attaches to the superscript "1" in "ZAR per kilogramme of gold¹" and clarifies that for **Tennant Mines** (an Australian operation) the per-kg metric is denominated in **A$**, not ZAR. **Implication**: Group AISC/AIC in ZAR/kg is a blended metric that mixes ZAR-denominated South African operations with an A$‑denominated Australian operation, translated at US$/A$ 0.68 (FY26) and 0.65 (FY25) `[D008.2, pp. 42–43]`. For forecasting, the Tennant A$ cost base must be translated at the A$–US$ rate, **not** the ZAR rate; a weaker A$ would reduce the US$ Group AISC. This is a hidden FX-direction risk: the US$/A$ moved 0.65→0.68 (+4.6% A$ strength), which *raised* Tennant's US$ cost contribution in FY26.

**Footnote 1 on p. 40 (table):** "*This total may not reflect the sum of the line items due to rounding.*" — Standard rounding caveat. Arithmetic check on the FY26 table: Barberton cash cost 146.2 + royalties 9.0 + community 1.1 − by-products 0.2 + corporate G&A 13.7 − reclamation 0.5 + sustaining dev 2.4 + sustaining maint 9.8 = **181.5** vs printed AISC **181.4** — 0.1 rounding difference, acceptable. Group: 271.6 + 73.8 + 72.6 = **418.0** but printed Group AISC **508.4** — the gap is the corporate G&A (34.9) and other reconciling items not obvious from the row layout; **flag**: the Group total column does not visibly foot from the mine columns as captured (boundary-crossing text may have dropped a column). Treat Group AISC 508.4 as the reported figure but verify the vertical arithmetic against the printed page.

**Footnote 1 on p. 41 (FY25 table):** "*The above table was disclosed in ZAR million in the 2024 integrated annual report.*" — **Critical**: the FY25 comparatives here are **restated/translated into US$** from the originally disclosed ZAR figures, using FY25 average US$/ZAR 18.17. This means FY25 AISC/AIC US$ figures are **not** the originally reported numbers and any year-on-year US$ comparison embeds FX translation. FY25 ZAR AISC was 934,517/kg → at 18.17 = ~US$1,600/oz (ties). **Forecast implication**: do not treat FY25 US$ AISC as a clean constant-currency base; use the ZAR/kg series (FY26 985,551 vs FY25 934,517, +5.5%) for like-for-like unit-cost trend, and apply the forecast FX separately.

**Boundary-crossing capture warning on p. 40 table:** The table's column layout is garbled (e.g. "19.6 61.7" on one row, "Total" columns interleaved). This is a **capture artefact, not a company error** — the mine-level sums (Barberton 181.4, Evander 90.2, BTRP+Elikhulu combined 73.8, MTR 72.6) should be read as the printed values but the Group 508.4 total needs visual re-verification against p. 40 of the PDF.

**Absence of a footnote on the "Voluntary severance package" line:** FY25 AIC includes a VSP line of **1.4** (Barberton) `[D008.2, p. 41]`; FY26 has **no** such line. This is a one-off FY25 item that inflated FY25 AIC but not FY26 — so the FY26 AIC increase (469.3→703.4, +50%) is **understated** on a clean basis. For forecasting FY27, assume no VSP unless a new restructuring is flagged (check D003 CEO statement / D009).

**AIC/AISC definitional caveat not footnoted but material:** AIC adds "non-sustaining capital expenditure **not associated with current operations**" `[D008.2, p. 40]`. The FY26 expansion capital of US$195.0m is **49.6% of Group AISC** (195.0/508.4) — i.e. roughly half of total all-in spend is growth capex. For FY27 forecasting this is the single largest swing factor: if expansion capital steps down (e.g. Evander 49.8 and Tennant 68.4 projects complete), AIC could fall sharply even with flat AISC.

## 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

No footnote markers (`[^1]`, `*`) or small-print qualifications appear in the captured p. 44 evidence. However, two embedded definitional qualifications carry forecast consequences:

**(a) Net debt definition — "before IFRS 9 accounting adjustments"** [D008.3, p. 44]
- The APM deliberately excludes IFRS 9 fair-value adjustments to borrowings and includes derivatives in cash equivalents. This means the (185.7) net cash figure is **not** the IFRS balance-sheet net debt; the reconciliation line "facility arrangement fees – (0.9 FY25)" is the only visible bridge item. Forecast implication: any DMTN/RCF fair-value movement will not flow into this APM — model net debt on **carrying borrowings less cash and ST investments**, and keep IFRS 9 adjustments as a separate reconciling item.

**(b) Net senior debt vs net debt** [p. 44]
- Net senior debt (194.7) excludes lease liabilities (7.8) and financial liabilities (1.2) but **still deducts** short-term investments (137.9). The 9.0 difference between net debt (185.7) and net senior debt (194.7) = 7.8 + 1.2 exactly. **Arithmetic check: passes.** This confirms both APMs embed the ST-investment deduction; covenant metrics built on "senior debt" will therefore benefit from the 137.9 ST investments.

**Arithmetic tensions flagged (verification required):**
- **FY26 net debt column sum**: (108.3) − 137.9 + 51.5 + 0 + 7.8 + 1.2 + 0 = **(185.7)** — ties.
- **FY25 net debt column sum**: (49.4) − 0 + 190.0 + 1.8 + 3.9 + 3.3 + 0.9 = **150.5** — ties.
- **Capex table**: Group total sustaining 21.8 (FY25 11.7); expansion 202.2 (FY25 156.2); total 224.0 (FY25 167.9). Check: 21.8 + 202.2 = 224.0 ✓; 11.7 + 156.2 = 167.9 ✓. **But** the printed column layout is garbled (FY25/FY26 headers interleaved), so individual mine rows should be re-verified against the printed page: e.g., "Barberton Mines total" sustaining 12.6, expansion 27.5 (FY25 19.1), total 40.1 (FY25 27.7) — 12.6 + 27.5 = 40.1 ✓. "Evander Mines total" sustaining 1.7, expansion 69.2 (FY25 45.9), total 70.9 (FY25 47.9) — 1.7 + 69.2 = 70.9 ✓. MTR total 32.6 = 2.7 + 29.9 ✓. Tennant total 71.3 = 2.9 + 68.4 ✓. **Group sum check**: 40.1 + 70.9 + 32.6 + 71.3 + 0.1 + 7.2 + 1.8 = 224.0 ✓. Capex table is internally consistent.
- **Sustaining + expansion vs "AIC expansion 195.0"**: 202.2 − 7.2 (solar) = **195.0** — exactly the AIC expansion capital figure. Strong confirmation AIC excludes corporate solar expansion. **New open question: confirm AIC expansion capital definition excludes solar and Agricultural ESG (0.1).**

Adjusted EBITDA is a measure of the Group’s operating performance and is calculated as net profit or loss for the 
Group before finance income and finance costs and tax, before any amount attributable to the amortisation of intangible 
assets and the depreciation of tangible assets and before any extraordinary items or the impairment of non-financial 
assets, bargain purchase gain and unrealised fair value loss on financial derivatives. A reconciliation of the Group’s 
adjusted EBITDA is provided in note 3 to the summarised financial statements.
A reconciliation of the adjusted EBITDA by operation has been provided below

--- Section D008.4 | original physical page 46 | text selection | Table ---
NET ADJUSTED EBITDA
Net adjusted EBITDA starts with adjusted EBITDA adjusted for any entries made to unrealised fair value gains or 
losses on financial derivatives that are entered into in the normal course of business as part of the Group’s financial risk 
management process.
FREE CASH FLOW
Free cash flow starts with adjusted EBITDA and is adjusted for changes in net working capital, non-cash flow items as 
determined by IAS 7, capital expenditure less capital funded through permitted indebtedness and tax payments.
HEADLINE EARNINGS
Headline earnings, a JSE-defined performance measure (as defined by Circular 2023/1 issued by SAICA), are reconciled 
from profit/(loss) after tax in note 13 to the summarised financial statements.
RATIOS
Return on shareholder funds
This ratio measures returns to equity shareholders as a percentage of the capital invested in the Group. It is calculated as 
profit/(loss) after tax expressed as a percentage of the average total equity for the current and previous reporting periods.
Net debt-to-equity ratio
This ratio measures the degree to which the Group finances its operations through debt relative to equity and is calculated 
as net debt divided by total equity.
Net debt-to-net adjusted EBITDA ratio
This ratio measures the number of years it would take the Group to repay its net debt from net adjusted EBITDA, 
assuming both variables are held consistent, and is calculated as net debt divided by net adjusted EBITDA.
Interest cover ratio
This ratio measures the Group’s ability to pay interest on its outstanding senior debt from net adjusted EBITDA and is 
calculated as total net adjusted EBITDA divided by finance costs incurred on interest-bearing debt.
Debt service cover ratio
This ratio measures the cash flow available for debt service relative to the Group’s obligatory principal and interest debt 
obligations and is calculated as free cash flow available for debt service divided by principal and interest-debt obligations

 GROSS PROFIT MARGIN

 CURRENT RATIO

## 1. Answers to Stored Questions for This Section

**Q110 (High) — EBITDA vs Adjusted EBITDA tables; reconcile 609,431**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D006 Note 3)`
- **Answer & evidence**: The by-operation Adjusted EBITDA reconciliation is printed `[D008.4, p. 45]`, but the **Group total of 663.1 does NOT tie to the 609.4 Adjusted EBITDA** used in Levered Free Cash Flow `[p. 46]`. The print gap is **53.7**. The reconciliation also presents a "Total" column of 602.2 for "Net income before finance income and finance costs" that does **not equal** the sum of the four operating columns (250.4 + 351.8 = 602.2, which ties, but the 250.4 and 351.8 sub-totals themselves are inconsistent with their constituent columns: Barberton 131.1 + Evander 119.4 = 250.5 ≠ 250.4; BTRP 27.5 + Elikhulu 156.8 = 184.3 ≠ 351.8, and adding MTR 140.3 + Tennant 27.1 = 351.7 ≠ 351.8). The **FY25 Group Adjusted EBITDA of 245.8** also differs from the **226.6** used in the LFCF table `[p. 46]`. Two different Group Adjusted EBITDA figures for the same year is a material reconciliation failure. **The 609.4 vs 663.1 / 226.6 vs 245.8 gap must be resolved to Note 3 segment analysis (D006.2) and Note 13 (D006.7)** — likely a corporate/head-office elimination line and/or an "unrealised fair value" treatment difference.
- **Remainder open**: Corporate cost line, elimination of intra-group, and the exact 53.7 (FY26) / 19.2 (FY25) bridge.

**Q27 (High) — Adjusted EBITDA definition change; SBP/Corporate 52,443**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open)`
- **Answer & evidence**: The D008.4 definition `[p. 45]` **explicitly excludes** "extraordinary items or the impairment of non-financial assets, bargain purchase gain and unrealised fair value loss on financial derivatives" but **does NOT mention share-based payment (SBP)**. Therefore SBP appears to remain **inside** Adjusted EBITDA unless Note 3 removes it. FY25 table confirms the mechanics: it starts from "EBITDA" then adds back unrealised FV loss (1.9) and bargain gain (28.0) to reach Adjusted EBITDA (245.8). No SBP line is shown as an add-back, which strongly implies **SBP is inside Adjusted EBITDA**. This is a **critical forecast issue**: if SBP (57,272/52,443 per stored Q28) sits inside Adjusted EBITDA, then Adjusted EBITDA overstates cash operating earnings and the FY27 forecast must deduct it. **Still open**: confirm in Note 3 (D006.2) whether SBP is a separate line inside the EBITDA reconciliation, and whether the 52,443 Corporate figure is SBP or corporate costs.

**Q28 (High) — Split Corporate 52,443 vs SBP 57,272**
- **Status**: `Still open`
- **Answer & evidence**: Not addressed in D008.4. The by-operation table `[p. 45]` has **no Corporate column** — only Barberton, Evander, BTRP, Elikhulu, MTR, Tennant. The 53.7 gap between 663.1 and 609.4 is the only place a corporate/elimination charge could hide, but it is not labelled. Carry to D006.2.

**Q36 (Low-Med) — Clean EBITDA bridge 609,431 vs 226,584**

The 609,431 and 226,584 absolute values are consistent with these (rounding). **However**, the by-operation table totals (663.1 / 245.8) do not tie, so the "clean" bridge is not yet verifiable. Carry to D006.2/Note 3.

**Q111 (High) — Adjusted EBITDA exclusions vs SBP 57,272**

- **Answer & evidence**: Definition `[p. 45]` excludes impairments, bargain purchase, unrealised FV losses on derivatives, D&A, finance and tax. **SBP is not listed as an exclusion** → likely inside Adjusted EBITDA. The FY25 reconciliation adds back only unrealised FV loss (1.9) and bargain gain (28.0). **Remainder open**: whether SBP 57,272 is embedded in operating costs within the 663.1/245.8 totals, and whether the 53.7/19.2 gaps represent corporate SBP.

**Q25 / Q127 / Q134 (High) — Debt metric, covenants, DMTN**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved`
- **Answer & evidence**: The ratios section `[p. 46]` defines:
  - **Net debt-to-net adjusted EBITDA** = net debt ÷ net adjusted EBITDA.
  - **Interest cover** = **total net adjusted EBITDA ÷ finance costs incurred on interest-bearing debt** (note: uses *net* adjusted EBITDA, and only interest-bearing debt finance costs — not total finance costs).
  - **Debt service cover** = FCF available for debt service ÷ principal and interest debt obligations.
  - **Net adjusted EBITDA** = adjusted EBITDA adjusted for unrealised FV gains/losses on financial derivatives `[p. 46]`.
  This clarifies **which EBITDA metric underpins covenants**: it is **net adjusted EBITDA**, not Adjusted EBITDA. **Still open**: the actual covenant levels/headroom, DMTN maturities, and RCF/ZAR availability — carry to D006.

## 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

**Footnote [^1] — page 46 (last traded price FX)**
> "Amounts converted at the 30 June 2026 closing exchange rate of US$/ZAR:16.46 (FY25: US$/ZAR:17.75)."
- **What it changes**: The last-traded-price of **125.09 US cents** (FY25 62.48) is a **closing-rate translation**, not an average. The cash flow yield per share of **7.96%** (FY25 3.75%) is therefore sensitive to the spot rate on 30 June 2026, not the FY26 average. **Do not use 16.46 as the FY27 budget FX rate** — it is a period-end spot rate. The ZAR strengthened from 17.75 to 16.46 (a ~7.3% ZAR appreciation), which **reduces the USD value of ZAR-denominated costs** — a tailwind to FY27 USD AISC that must be verified against the FY27 guidance FX deck (still open, Q125).
- **Verification**: Confirm the FY27 guidance FX assumption in D002/D003/D009 — if guidance uses a weaker ZAR (e.g., 17.5–18.0), the FY27 AISC guidance of 2,075–2,175/oz may embed a different FX than the 16.46 spot.

**Footnote [^1] — page 47 (dividend)**
> "Includes the maiden interim dividend of ZA 12.00 cents (0.55 pence) per share."
- **What it changes**: FY26 DPS of **77.00 cents** includes a **maiden interim of 12.00 cents** — this is a **structural change to dividend policy** (from annual to semi-annual). The FY26 total of 77.00 vs FY25 37.00 is a 108% increase. The interim component implies the FY27 dividend will be split into two payments, affecting **cash flow timing** (interim paid ~April, final ~October). For the FY27 forecast, model **two dividend cash outflows**, not one.
- **Hidden implication**: The maiden interim signals management confidence in recurring cash flow — but the **77% LFCF payout ratio** (156m dividend / 201.8m LFCF) is high and leaves limited buffer if the +46.6 WC inflow reverses.
- **Verification**: Check D005.1 (statement of changes in equity) and D006 for the actual dividend paid in cash vs declared, and whether the interim was declared post-year-end (a liability at 30 June 2026).

**Embedded qualification — "Total" column arithmetic (page 45)**
- The "Total" column of **602.2** for "Net income before finance income and finance costs" is presented as a Group total, but the sub-totals **250.4** (Barberton+Evander: 131.1+119.4 = 250.5) and **351.8** (BTRP+Elikhulu+MTR+Tennant: 27.5+156.8+140.3+27.1 = 351.7) each differ by 0.1 from their components. **602.2 = 250.4 + 351.8** ties at the sub-total level, but the sub-totals do not tie to their columns. This is either a **rounding presentation** (each mine rounded to 0.1) or a **real reconciliation gap**. The larger issue is the **Group Adjusted EBITDA of 663.1 vs 609.4** — a 53.7 gap with **no printed reconciliation line** (no Corporate, no eliminations, no SBP line). This is the single most important footnote-adjacent anomaly in the section: **the by-operation table and the LFCF table use two different Group Adjusted EBITDA figures for the same year**. Until Note 3 reconciles them, the FY27 Adjusted EBITDA forecast cannot be anchored.
- **Verification**: D006.2 (Note 3 Segment Analysis) — demand a printed line-by-line bridge from 663.1 to 609.4 (and 245.8 to 226.6), identifying corporate costs, SBP, and any elimination.

**Embedded qualification — "Net adjusted EBITDA" (page 46)**
- "Net adjusted EBITDA starts with adjusted EBITDA adjusted for any entries made to unrealised fair value gains or losses on financial derivatives that are entered into in the normal course of business as part of the Group's financial risk management process."
- **What it changes**: Covenants and the net debt-to-net adjusted EBITDA ratio use **net adjusted EBITDA**, not Adjusted EBITDA. For FY26, the adjustment is likely small (FY25 was 1.9), but for FY27, **hedging gains/losses could be material** given the gold price volatility. A large unrealised hedging loss would reduce net adjusted EBITDA and **tighten the leverage covenant** — a forecast risk not visible in the headline 609.4.
- **Verification**: D006 — locate the FY26 unrealised FV movement on derivatives and any FY27 hedge book mark-to-market.

**Embedded qualification — Interest cover definition (page 46)**
- "total net adjusted EBITDA divided by finance costs incurred on **interest-bearing debt**."
- **What it changes**: Interest cover uses **only interest-bearing debt finance costs**, excluding the ~2,813 non-borrowing finance cost gap (Q31/Q38). This means the covenant ratio is **more favourable** than a total-finance-cost cover would be. The FY26 finance costs paid of 15.0 `[p. 46]` is the closest proxy for the interest-bearing component. **For forecasting, the covenant headroom is wider than the P&L finance cost suggests** — but confirm the exact interest-bearing finance cost quantum in D006.

**Embedded qualification — FCF definition "less capital funded through permitted indebtedness" (page 46)**
- **What it changes**: FCF and LFCF **deduct capex only to the extent not funded by permitted indebtedness**. If a large expansion project (e.g., solar) is funded by permitted debt, the capex does **not** reduce LFCF, flattering the 201.8 LFCF. **The quantum of permitted-indebtedness-funded capex is not printed** — this is a material omission. If, say, 30–50m of FY26 capex was debt-funded, the "true" unflattered LFCF would be materially lower.
- **Verification**: D006 (debt note/covenants) — identify the permitted-indebtedness capex amount and which project it funds.

**Embedded qualification — Large working capital inflow (page 46)**
- "Net working capital movement **+46.6**" (FY25 (2.0)).
- **What it changes**: The +46.6 WC inflow is **~23% of LFCF (201.8)**. A one-off payables stretch or receivable release of this size is **unlikely to repeat** in FY27. **Forecast LFCF ex-WC is closer to ~155m**, not 201.8. This is a critical normalization adjustment.
- **Verification**: D005/D006 — decompose the 46.6 into receivables, inventory, and payables movements; check whether payables (126,369 per stored Q20) stretched.

## D009 Analysis — Group Production Summary (pp. 48–50)

### 1. Answers to Stored Questions for This Section

**Q34: Tennant tonnes/grade/recovery**
- **Status**: `Resolved (with caveat on A$ vs US$ reconciliation)`
- **Answer & evidence**: Tennant (MTR operation) FY26 tonnes milled: underground 0 (no underground tonnes shown for MTR), surface feedstock processed 751,914 t (vs 85,316 t FY25) `[D009, p.48]`. Head grade (total) 1.4 g/t (vs 1.3 g/t FY25); excluding third-party buy-in feedstock 1.4 g/t (vs 1.3) `[D009, p.48]`. Overall recovered grade 1.3 g/t (vs 0.6 g/t FY25). Overall recovery — surface feedstock 94% (vs 43% FY25); tailings 94% (vs 43%) `[D009, p.48]`. Gold produced — surface feedstock 32,124 oz (vs 1,513 oz) — this is the 21× step-up in Tennant output. **Caveat**: the tailings row for Tennant shows 94% recovery but 0 tailings tonnes processed for MTR (the 94% appears in the surface-feedstock column), suggesting a table-layout artefact; verify against the MTR operational commentary in D003. Also A$ revenue 195.3m / A$ cost 81.0m / A$ AISC 106.8m / A$ AIC 209.3m for Tennant; A$ cash cost 81,099/kg and A$ AISC 106,900/kg `[D009, p.49]` — these need FX/A$ reconciliation in D008 if a US$ bridge is required.

**Q86/Q32/Q40: Emmerson nil FY27 production/revenue confirmation**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (nil production confirmed; revenue/cost run-rate not addressed here)`
- **Answer & evidence**: The D009 tables contain **no Emmerson column or row at all** — production, tonnes, revenue, cost and AISC tables are limited to Barberton, Evander, BTRP, Elikhulu, MTR and Tennant `[D009, pp.48–50]`. This is consistent with (but does not explicitly state) nil FY27 Emmerson production/revenue. **Remainder stays open**: confirm in D006 Note 3 segment analysis that Emmerson carries zero revenue/cost and that any care-and-maintenance cost is in Corporate, not a mining segment.

**Q107: Elikhulu throughput/power-cost decomposition**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (throughput resolved; power-cost decomposition NOT in D009)`
- **Answer & evidence**: Elikhulu tonnes processed — tailings FY26 13,807,025 t (vs 14,747,232 t FY25), a 6.4% decline `[D009, p.48]`. Head grade 0.3 g/t flat; overall recovery 39% (vs 35%); gold produced — tailings 56,475 oz (vs 52,606 oz); gold sold 57,092 oz `[D009, p.49]`. US$ cash cost 1,081/oz (vs 1,007); US$ AISC 1,231/oz (vs 1,077); US$ AIC 1,571/oz (vs 1,172) `[D009, p.49]`. ZAR cash cost/t 76 (vs 66). **Power-cost decomposition is NOT disclosed in D009** — no electricity line, no unit power cost. Remainder stays open for D003/D008.

**Q113: By-product credits commodity/price & Tennant tie**
- **Status**: `Not resolved in D009 — no by-product credit line present`
- **Answer & evidence**: D009 discloses no by-product credit line, no commodity breakdown (e.g., uranium, sulphuric acid, silver) and no price assumptions. The only "credits" proxy is the "US$ AISC – excluding third-party buy-in feedstock" row (Group 1,798 vs 1,867) `[D009, p.49]`, which is a feedstock exclusion, not a by-product credit. **Carry forward** to D008 (APM reconciliations) and D006 (revenue note).

**Q123: Barberton higher-cost bucket confirmation**
- **Status**: `Resolved / confirmed`
- **Answer & evidence**: Barberton Mines US$ AISC FY26 **2,589/oz** (vs 2,065 FY25) and US$ AIC **2,950/oz** (vs 2,322) `[D009, p.49]` — confirmed as the highest-cost underground mine, above Evander (1,914) and well above surface ops. Barberton ZAR cash cost/kg 1,133,459 (vs 1,009,725), ZAR AISC/kg 1,406,730 (vs 1,206,373). Driver visible: tonnes milled total 342,688 t (vs 331,964) but underground tonnes 280,412 (vs 266,676) and surface 62,276 (vs 65,288); head grade 7.0 g/t (vs 7.3) — grade decline plus cost inflation. The ~US$3,487/oz "higher-cost bucket" in the D008 APM is consistent with Barberton AISC 2,589 plus other higher-cost ounces; confirm exact bucket composition in D008.3 if printed.

**Q130: Mine sustaining capex tie**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (capex by mine captured; sustaining vs expansion split NOT shown in D009)`
- **Answer & evidence**: D009 capital expenditure (US$m) FY26: Barberton 37.5, Evander 49.8, BTRP 2.6, Elikhulu 21.1, MTR 32.6, Tennant 71.3; Barberton total 40.1, Evander total 70.9, surface total 127.6; Group total **214.9** (vs 163.6 FY25) `[D009, p.50]`. **D009 does not split sustaining vs expansion** — so the D008.3 sustaining-capital rows (e.g., Barberton, Evander) cannot be tied from D009 alone. **Remainder stays open** for D008.3 / D006 Note 7: reconcile D009 total capex 214.9 to D008 total capex 224.0 (a 9.1 gap) and to PP&E additions.

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

- **No footnote markers (`[^1]`, `*`) are present in the supplied D009 evidence** on pp. 48–50. The tables carry no printed footnote definitions. I will not fabricate any; if the printed page carries footnotes outside the captured area, they are missing from this capture — verification question logged below (Q135).
- **Embedded qualifications that change interpretation**:
  1. **"US$ AISC – excluding third-party buy-in feedstock"** row `[D009, p.49]`: Group 1,798 vs headline 1,867; Barberton 2,570 vs 2,589; Evander 1,698 vs 1,914; BTRP 1,529 vs 1,564; Elikhulu unchanged 1,231; MTR 1,386 vs 1,389; Tennant unchanged 2,261. This tells us the **headline AISC is inflated by third-party buy-in feed** particularly at Evander (216/oz effect) and Barberton (19/oz). For forecasting, the buy-in feedstock is a margin pass-through, not a cost base — model it separately.
  2. **Tennant A$ vs US$ cost columns** `[D009, pp.49–50]`: A$ cash cost 81,099/kg, A$ AISC 106,900/kg, A$ AIC 205,143/kg vs US$ cash 1,715/oz, AISC 2,261, AIC 4,339. The **AIC/cash-cost ratio at Tennant is extreme (4,339/1,715 ≈ 2.53×)** vs Group (2,576/1,600 ≈ 1.61×), driven by expansion capital (Tennant capex 71.3 of Group 214.9). This is a step-change signal: Tennant is in a heavy expansion phase; forecast AIC must carry this capital load.
  3. **Table-layout inconsistency** in the "Tonnes processed – tailings" and "Tonnes milled and processed – total" rows on p.48: the FY26 tailings row shows "13,807,025  11,566,768" and "26,149,178" in a way that does not cleanly map to BTRP/Elikhulu/MTR columns; the FY26 total row (27,584,049 / 1,191,445 / 14,124,322 / 12,178,487) and the FY25 equivalents (23,067,608 / 1,057,499 / 14,903,851 / 7,509,525) contain duplicated/merged values. **This is a capture/printing artefact** — flagged as a verification question, not used as a hard input. The clean figures used: BTRP 775,385 t; Elikhulu 13,807,025 t; MTR 11,566,768 t (FY26), summing to 26,149,178 t tailings processed.
  4. **Tennant "Gold produced – tailings 94% recovery" with zero tailings tonnes** — the 94% in the tailings row for MTR appears to be the surface-feedstock recovery mis-slotted; use the surface-feedstock row (751,914 t, 1.4 g/t, 94%, 32,124 oz) as the operative Tennant feed.
  5. **Gold produced vs gold sold reconciliation**: Group produced FY26 **272,310 oz** vs sold **272,373 oz** `[D009, p.49]` — sold exceeds produced by 63 oz (minor inventory draw). FY25 produced 196,527 vs sold 196,926 (sold exceeds by 399). Use **sold** for revenue forecasting; produced for cost-per-oz.
  6. **FY25 Evander underground gold produced** shows 26,748 in the underground row but 27,829 in the "Evander Mines total" column `[D009, p.48]` — a 1,081 oz difference equal to the third-party buy-in feedstock ounces. The total column is the right figure.
